\displaystyle\text{ for all }a\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times},\,\gamma\in\Gamma_{1}(p^{j}\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{w}\backslash\Gamma_{0}(p\mathbb{Z}_{p})\big\}.

The objects
\mathcal{A}_{\nu,i,j}^{\cdot}
can be regarded as left
\mathcal{O}[\Sigma^{\cdot}_{0}(p)]
-modules. Let
\mathcal{A}_{\nu,i}^{\cdot}=\varinjlim_{j}\mathcal{A}_{\nu,i,j}^{\cdot}
. Then
\mathcal{A}_{\nu}^{\cdot}\cong\varprojlim_{i}\mathcal{A}_{\nu,i}^{\cdot}
. We denote by
\bm{\mathcal{A}}_{\nu}^{\cdot}
the object in
\mathbf{S}(Y_{\et})
corresponding to
\{\mathcal{A}_{\nu,i}^{\cdot}\}_{i}\in\mathbf{M}(\Gamma_{0}(p\mathbb{Z}_{p}))
. We also define
\mathcal{D}_{\nu,i}^{\cdot}=\Hom_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{A}_{\nu,i,i}^{\cdot},\mathcal{O}/\mathfrak{m}^{i})
. These objects can be regarded as right
\mathcal{O}[\Sigma^{\cdot}_{0}(p)]
-modules and we have
\mathcal{D}_{\nu}^{\cdot}\cong\varprojlim_{i}\mathcal{D}_{\nu,i}^{\cdot}
. We denote by
\bm{\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}^{\cdot}
the object in
\mathbf{S}(Y_{\et})
corresponding to
\{\mathcal{D}_{\nu,i}^{\cdot}\}_{i}\in\mathbf{M}(\Gamma_{0}(p\mathbb{Z}_{p}))
. There are natural morphisms of
\mathcal{O}
-modules

H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\bm{\mathcal{A}}_{\nu}^{\cdot})\rightarrow\mathtt{H}^{1}_{\et}(Y_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\bm{\mathcal{A}}_{\nu}^{\cdot})\cong H^{1}(\Gamma,\mathcal{A}_{\nu}^{\cdot})

and

H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\bm{\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}^{\cdot})\cong\mathtt{H}^{1}_{\et}(Y_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\bm{\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}^{\cdot})\cong H^{1}(\Gamma,\mathcal{D}_{\nu}^{\cdot})

compatible with the action of Hecke operators. We also have Hecke-equivariant isomorphisms

H^{1}_{\et,c}(Y_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\bm{\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}^{\cdot})\cong\mathtt{H}^{1}_{\et,c}(Y_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\bm{\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}^{\cdot})\cong H^{1}_{c}(\Gamma,\mathcal{D}_{\nu}^{\cdot}),

where
H^{j}_{c}(\Gamma,-)=H^{j-1}(\Gamma,\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}}(\text{Div}^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{Q})),-))
. These isomorphisms
allow us to define continuous
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-actions on the groups
H^{1}(\Gamma,\mathcal{A}_{\nu}^{\cdot})
,
H^{1}(\Gamma,\mathcal{D}_{\nu}^{\cdot})
and
H^{1}_{c}(\Gamma,\mathcal{D}_{\nu}^{\cdot})
.

Given a character
\chi:\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}^{\times}
, let
\mathcal{O}(\chi)
be the module
\mathcal{O}
with
\Gamma_{0}(p\mathbb{Z}_{p})
acting via
\chi\circ\det
, where
\det:\Gamma_{0}(p\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
is the determinant map.

The natural
\mathcal{G}
-equivariant evaluation map
\mathcal{A}_{\nu}^{\cdot}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\mathcal{D}_{\nu}^{\cdot}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}
yields a
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-equivariant cup-product pairing

(5.2)

H^{1}(\Gamma,\mathcal{A}_{\nu}^{\cdot})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}H^{1}_{c}(\Gamma,\mathcal{D}_{\nu}^{\cdot})\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}(-1)

under which the Hecke operators
T_{q}
,
T_{q}^{\prime}
,
[d]_{N}
,
[d]_{N}^{\prime}
acting covariantly on the left, whenever defined, are adjoint to these same operators acting contravariantly on the right.

Let
\det:\mathsf{T}^{\prime}\times\mathsf{T}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
be the function defined by
\det((x_{1},x_{2}),(y_{1},y_{2}))=x_{1}y_{2}-x_{2}y_{1}
and let
\det_{\nu}
be the composition of this function with
\nu:\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}
. Evaluation at this function defines a
\mathcal{G}
-equivariant map
\mathcal{D}_{\nu}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\mathcal{D}_{\nu}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(-\nu)
which yields a
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-equivariant cup-product pairing

(5.3)

H^{1}(\Gamma,\mathcal{D}_{\nu}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}H^{1}_{c}(\Gamma,\mathcal{D}_{\nu})\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}(\bm{\nu})(-1),

where
\bm{\nu}=\nu\circ\epsilon_{\text{cyc}}:G_{\mathbb{Q}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}^{\times}
. Under this pairing, the Hecke operators
T_{q}
,
T_{q}^{\prime}
,
[d]_{N}
,
[d]_{N}^{\prime}
acting contravariantly on the left, whenever defined, are adjoint to the Hecke operators
T_{q}^{\prime}
,
T_{q}
,
[d]_{N}^{\prime}
,
[d]_{N}
acting contravariantly on the right. We obtain a similar pairing interchanging the roles of
\mathcal{D}_{\nu}
and
\mathcal{D}_{\nu}^{\prime}
.

5.4.
Ordinary cohomology

For any
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-algebra
B
, let
S_{r}(B)
be the set of two-variable homogeneous polynomials of degree
r
in
B[x_{1},x_{2}]
. It is a left
B[\Sigma_{0}^{\cdot}(p)]
-module with the action of
\Sigma_{0}^{\cdot}(p)
defined by

gP(x_{1},x_{2})=P((x_{1},x_{2})\cdot g)

for all
g\in\Sigma_{0}^{\cdot}(p)
and
P(x_{1},x_{2})\in S_{r}(B)
. To the
p
-adic
\Gamma_{0}(p\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-representation
S_{r}=S_{r}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
there corresponds the locally contant
p
-adic sheaf
\mathscr{S}_{r}
on
Y_{\et}
defined in
\lx@sectionsign\ref{subsubsec:relT}
. Therefore we have an isomorphism

H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathscr{S}_{r})\cong H^{1}(\Gamma,S_{r})

which is Hecke-equivariant when we consider the covariant action of Hecke operators on both sides, and we use this isomorphism to define an action of
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
on
H^{1}(\Gamma,S_{r})
.

We also define
L_{r}(B)=\Hom_{B}(S_{r}(B),B)
, which we regard as a right
B[\Sigma_{0}^{\cdot}(p)]
-module defining the
\Sigma_{0}^{\cdot}(p)
-action by

(\mu\cdot g)(P(x_{1},x_{2}))=\mu(gP(x_{1},x_{2}))

for all
g\in\Sigma_{0}^{\cdot}(p)
,
\mu\in L_{r}(B)
and
P(x_{1},x_{2})\in S_{r}(B)
. To the
p
-adic
\Gamma_{0}(p\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-representation
L_{r}=L_{r}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
there corresponds the locally constant
p
-adic sheaf
\mathscr{L}_{r}
on
Y_{\et}
. Therefore we have an isomorphism

H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathscr{L}_{r})\cong H^{1}(\Gamma,L_{r})

which is Hecke-equivariant when we consider the contravariant action of Hecke operators on both sides, and we use this isomorphism to define an action of
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
on
H^{1}(\Gamma,L_{r})
.

The natural
\Gamma_{0}(p\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-equivariant evaluation map
S_{r}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}L_{r}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}
yields a
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-equivariant cup-product pairing

(5.4)

H^{1}(\Gamma,S_{r})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}H^{1}_{c}(\Gamma,L_{r})\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}(-1)

under which the Hecke operators
T_{q}
,
T_{q}^{\prime}
,
[d]_{N}
,
[d]_{N}^{\prime}
acting covariantly on the left, whenever defined, are adjoint to these same operators acting contravariantly on the right. This pairing becomes perfect after inverting
p
.

Let
\nu_{r}:\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
be the character defined by
\nu_{r}(z)=z^{r}
. Evaluation at the polynomial
(x_{1}y_{2}-x_{2}y_{1})^{r}\in S_{r}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}S_{r}
defines a
\Gamma_{0}(p\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-equivariant map
L_{r}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}L_{r}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}(-\nu_{r})
and thus yields a
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-equivariant cup-product pairing

(5.5)

H^{1}(\Gamma,L_{r})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}H^{1}_{c}(\Gamma,L_{r})\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}(r-1)

under which the Hecke operators
T_{q}
,
T_{q}^{\prime}
,
[d]_{N}
,
[d]_{N}^{\prime}
acting contravariantly on the left, whenever defined, are adjoint to the Hecke operators
T_{q}^{\prime}
,
T_{q}
,
[d]_{N}^{\prime}
,
[d]_{N}
acting contravariantly on the right. This pairing becomes perfect after inverting
p
.

Combining these two pairings we can define a morphism

\mathtt{s}_{r\ast}:H^{1}(\Gamma,S_{r}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}))\longrightarrow H^{1}(\Gamma,L_{r}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}))(-r).

This map is
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-equivariant and intertwines the covariant action of the operators
T_{q}
,
[d]_{N}
,
[a]_{p}
on the source with the contravariant action of the operators
T_{q}^{\prime}
,
[d]_{N}^{\prime}
,
[a]_{p}^{\prime}
on the target.
We can also define
\mathtt{s}_{r\ast}
directly via the isomorphism
S_{r}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cong L_{r}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})(\nu_{r})
arising from the perfect pairing
L_{r}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}L_{r}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}(-\nu_{r})
defined by evaluation at
(x_{1}y_{2}-x_{2}y_{1})^{r}
. Therefore, the denominators introduced by this map are bounded by
r!
, i.e., an element in

\im\left(H^{1}(\Gamma,S_{r})\rightarrow H^{1}(\Gamma,S_{r}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}))\right)

is mapped to an element in

\frac{1}{r!}\im\left(H^{1}(\Gamma,L_{r})\rightarrow H^{1}(\Gamma,L_{r}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}))\right),

as follows from
[
BSV22
, Rmk. 3.3]
.

To slightly simplify the notation, we will write
\mathcal{A}_{r}^{\cdot}
and
\mathcal{D}_{r}^{\cdot}
for
\mathcal{A}_{\nu_{r}}^{\cdot}
and
\mathcal{D}_{\nu_{r}}^{\cdot}
, respectively. Regarding two-variable polynomials as functions on
\mathsf{T}^{\cdot}
, we obtain a natural morphism of left
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\Sigma_{0}^{\cdot}(p)]
-modules
S_{r}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{r}^{\cdot}
. Also, dualizing this map, we obtain a morphism of right
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\Sigma_{0}^{\cdot}(p)]
-modules
\mathcal{D}_{r}^{\cdot}\rightarrow L_{r}
. Thus, we have
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-equivariant and Hecke-equivariant morphisms

H^{1}(\Gamma,S_{r})\rightarrow H^{1}(\Gamma,\mathcal{A}_{r}^{\cdot})\quad\text{and}\quad H^{1}(\Gamma,\mathcal{D}_{r}^{\cdot})\rightarrow H^{1}(\Gamma,L_{r}).

Applying Hida’s (anti-)ordinary projector
e_{\text{ord}}^{\cdot}:=\lim_{n\to\infty}(T_{p}^{\cdot})^{n!}
, the previous morphisms become isomorphisms

e_{\text{ord}}^{\cdot}H^{1}(\Gamma,S_{r})\cong e_{\text{ord}}^{\cdot}H^{1}(\Gamma,\mathcal{A}_{r}^{\cdot}),\quad e_{\text{ord}}^{\cdot}H^{1}(\Gamma,\mathcal{D}_{r}^{\cdot})\cong e_{\text{ord}}^{\cdot}H^{1}(\Gamma,L_{r}).

Under these isomorphisms, the pairings (
5.4
) and (
5.5
) correspond to the pairings (
5.2
) and (
5.3
), respectively, after applying the corresponding (anti-)ordinary projector to every term involved.

5.5.
\Lambda
-adic Poincaré pairing

It will be convenient to write
\langle a;b\rangle
, with
a\in(\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z})^{\times}
and
b\in(\mathbb{Z}/p^{r}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}
, for the diamond operator
\langle d\rangle
, where
d\in(\mathbb{Z}/Np^{r})^{\times}
is congruent to
a
modulo
N
and to
b
modulo
p^{r}
. We also write
\epsilon_{N}:G_{\mathbb{Q}}\rightarrow(\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z})^{\times}
for the mod
N
cyclotomic character.

For any positive integer
r
, let

G_{r}=1+p(\mathbb{Z}/p^{r}\mathbb{Z}),\quad\tilde{G}_{r}=(\mathbb{Z}/p^{r}\mathbb{Z})^{\times},

and define

\Lambda_{r}=\mathbb{Z}_{p}[G_{r}],\quad\tilde{\Lambda}_{r}=\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\tilde{G}_{r}],\quad\Lambda=\varprojlim_{r}\Lambda_{r}=\mathbb{Z}_{p}[[1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p}]],\quad\tilde{\Lambda}=\varprojlim_{r}\tilde{\Lambda}_{r}=\mathbb{Z}_{p}[[\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}]].

We have natural factorizations
(\mathbb{Z}/p^{r}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}=\mu_{p-1}\times(1+p\mathbb{Z}/p^{r}\mathbb{Z})
and
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}=\mu_{p-1}\times(1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p})
which give natural embeddings
\Lambda_{r}\xhookrightarrow{}\tilde{\Lambda}_{r}
and
\Lambda\xhookrightarrow{}\tilde{\Lambda}
. We define idempotents

e_{i}=\frac{1}{p-1}\sum_{\zeta\in\mu_{p-1}}\zeta^{-i}[\zeta]

for any integer
i
modulo
p-1
. Let
\kappa_{i}:\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\rightarrow\Lambda^{\times}
be the character defined by
z\mapsto\omega^{i}(z)[\langle z\rangle]
and let
\bm{\kappa}_{i}=\kappa_{i}\circ\epsilon_{\text{cyc}}:G_{\mathbb{Q}}\rightarrow\Lambda^{\times}
.

We will shorten notation by writing

(5.6)

X_{r}(m)=X(1,Np^{r}(m)),\quad H^{1}_{\et}(X_{\infty}(m)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})=\varprojlim_{r}H^{1}_{\et}(X_{r}(m)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathbb{Z}_{p}).

We have a natural action of
\tilde{\Lambda}_{r}
and
\tilde{\Lambda}
on the previous groups defined by letting group-like elements
[u]
act like the diamond operators
\langle 1;u\rangle^{\prime}
.

Fix compatible primitive
p
-power roots of unity
\zeta_{p^{r}}
and a primitive
N
-th root of unity
\zeta_{N}
. Then one can define Atkin–Lehner automorphisms
w_{r}
and
w
for the curve
X_{r}(m)
similarly as in
[
DR17
, §1.2]
. More precisely,
X_{r}(m)
parameterizes quadruples
(E,P,Q,C)
, where
E
is an elliptic curve,
P
is a point of order
N
,
Q
is a point of order
p^{r}
and
C
is a cyclic subgroup of
E
of order
Nm
containing
P
. Then, we define

w_{r}(E,P,Q,C)=(E/C_{Q},P+C_{Q},Q^{\prime}+C_{Q},C+C_{Q}/C_{Q}),

where
C_{Q}\subseteq E
is the subgroup generated by
Q
and
Q^{\prime}\in E[p^{r}]
satisfies
\langle Q,Q^{\prime}\rangle=\zeta_{p^{r}}
. Similarly, we define

w(E,P,Q,C)=(E/C,P^{\prime}+C,Q+C,E[Nm]/C),

where
P^{\prime}\in E[N]
satisfies
\langle P,P^{\prime}\rangle=\zeta_{N}
. These Atkin–Lehner automorphisms satisfy, for any
\sigma\in G_{\mathbb{Q}}
,

w_{r}^{\sigma}=\langle 1;\epsilon_{\text{cyc}}(\sigma)\rangle w_{r},\quad w^{\sigma}=\langle\epsilon_{N}(\sigma);1\rangle w.

We let
w
and
w_{r}
act on cohomology via pullback.

Define
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-equivariant pairings

\langle\,,\rangle_{G_{r}}:e_{i}H^{1}_{\et}(X_{r}(m)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})\times e_{-i}H^{1}_{\et}(X_{r}(m)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})\longrightarrow\Lambda_{r}(-1)

by the formula

\langle a,b\rangle_{G_{r}}=\sum_{\sigma\in G_{r}}\langle a^{\sigma},b\rangle_{r}\cdot\sigma^{-1},

where
\langle\,,\rangle_{r}
stands for the natural Poincaré pairing. These pairings are
\Lambda_{r}
-linear and anti-linear in the first and second argument, respectively. Then we get
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-equivariant
\Lambda_{r}
-pairings

[\,,]_{G_{r}}:e_{i}H^{1}_{\et}(X_{r}(m)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})\times e_{i}H^{1}_{\et}(X_{r}(m)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})(\langle\epsilon_{N}^{-1};1\rangle^{\prime})\longrightarrow\Lambda_{r}(\bm{\kappa}_{i})(-1)

via the following modification of the previous pairing:

[a,b]_{G_{r}}=\langle a,ww_{r}\cdot(T_{p}^{\prime})^{r}\cdot b\rangle_{G_{r}}.

These pairings are compatible in the sense that the diagram
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{i}H^{1}_{\et}(X_{r+1}(m)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})\times e_{i}H^{1}_{\et}(X_{r+1}(m)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})(\langle\epsilon_{N}^{-1};1\rangle^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda_{r+1}(\bm{\kappa}_{i})(-1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{i}H^{1}_{\et}(X_{r}(m)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})\times e_{i}H^{1}_{\et}(X_{r}(m)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})(\langle\epsilon_{N}^{-1};1\rangle^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda_{r}(\bm{\kappa}_{i})(-1)}
[\,,]_{G_{r+1}}
\pi_{1\ast}\times\pi_{1\ast}
[\,,]_{G_{r}}

commutes, which can be proved as in
[
DR17
, Lem. 1.1]
. This yields a
\Lambda
-adic perfect
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-equivariant pairing

(5.7)

e_{i}H^{1}_{\et}(X_{\infty}(m)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\text{ord}}\times e_{i}H^{1}_{\et}(X_{\infty}(m)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\text{ord}}(\langle\epsilon_{N}^{-1};1\rangle^{\prime})\longrightarrow\Lambda(\bm{\kappa}_{i})(-1),

where
H^{1}_{\et}(X_{\infty}(m)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\text{ord}}=e_{\text{ord}}^{\prime}H^{1}_{\et}(X_{\infty}(m)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. All Hecke operators are self-adjoint for this pairing.

5.6.
Big Galois representations

Let
\mathfrak{m}_{\Lambda}
be the maximal ideal of
\Lambda
, let
\text{Cont}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\Lambda)
be the
\Lambda
-module of continuous functions on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
with values in
\Lambda
, and let
\kappa
be any of the
\kappa_{i}
above. Define the
\Lambda
-module

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\kappa}^{\prime}=\big\{f:\mathsf{T}^{\prime}\rightarrow\Lambda\;|\;f(pz,1)\in\text{Cont}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\Lambda)\text{ and }f(a\cdot\gamma)=\kappa(a)\cdot f(\gamma)\text{ for all }a\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times},\,\gamma\in\mathsf{T}^{\prime}\big\},

equipped with the
\mathfrak{m}_{\Lambda}
-adic topology, and the
\Lambda
-module

\mathcal{D}_{\kappa}^{\prime}=\Hom_{\text{cont},\Lambda}(\mathcal{A}_{\kappa}^{\prime},\Lambda)

equipped with the weak-
\ast
topology. As in
\lx@sectionsign\ref{subsubsec:dist}
, we can regard
\mathcal{A}_{\kappa}^{\prime}
(respectively
\mathcal{D}_{\kappa}^{\prime}
) as a left (respectively right)
\Lambda[\Sigma^{\prime}_{0}(p)]
-module.

Similarly to what we did in
\lx@sectionsign\ref{subsubsec:group-et}
, define, for any positive integers
j,r
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\kappa,j,r}^{\prime}=\big\{f:\Gamma_{1}(p^{r}\mathbb{Z}_{p})\backslash\Gamma_{0}(p\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rightarrow\Lambda/\mathfrak{m}_{\Lambda}^{j}\;|\;f(a\cdot\gamma)=\kappa(a)\cdot f(\gamma)

\displaystyle\text{ for all }a\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times},\,\gamma\in\Gamma_{1}(p^{r}\mathbb{Z}_{p})\backslash\Gamma_{0}(p\mathbb{Z}_{p})\big\}

and
\mathcal{A}_{\kappa,j}^{\prime}=\varinjlim_{r}\mathcal{A}_{\kappa,j,r}^{\prime}
. Then
\mathcal{A}_{\kappa}^{\prime}=\varprojlim_{j}\mathcal{A}_{\kappa,j}^{\prime}
. We denote by
\bm{\mathcal{A}}_{\kappa}^{\prime}
the object in
\mathbf{S}(Y_{\et})
corresponding to
\{\mathcal{A}_{\kappa,j}^{\prime}\}_{j}\in\mathbf{M}(\Gamma_{0}(p\mathbb{Z}_{p}))
. We also define
\mathcal{D}_{\kappa,j}^{\prime}=\Hom_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{A}_{\kappa,j,j}^{\prime},\Lambda/\mathfrak{m}_{\Lambda}^{j})
, so that
\mathcal{D}_{\kappa}^{\prime}=\varprojlim_{j}\mathcal{D}_{\kappa,j}^{\prime}
, and denote by
\bm{\mathcal{D}}_{\kappa}^{\prime}
the object in
\mathbf{S}(Y_{\et})
corresponding to
\{\mathcal{D}_{\kappa,j}^{\prime}\}_{j}\in\mathbf{M}(\Gamma_{0}(p\mathbb{Z}_{p}))
. There are natural Hecke-equivariant morphisms of
\Lambda
-modules

H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\bm{\mathcal{A}}_{\kappa}^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathtt{H}^{1}_{\et}(Y_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\bm{\mathcal{A}}_{\kappa}^{\prime})\cong H^{1}(\Gamma,\mathcal{A}_{\kappa}^{\prime}),

H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\bm{\mathcal{D}}_{\kappa}^{\prime})\cong\mathtt{H}^{1}_{\et}(Y_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\bm{\mathcal{D}}_{\kappa}^{\prime})\cong H^{1}(\Gamma,\mathcal{D}_{\kappa}^{\prime}),

H^{1}_{\et,c}(Y_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\bm{\mathcal{D}}_{\kappa}^{\prime})\cong\mathtt{H}^{1}_{\et,c}(Y_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\bm{\mathcal{D}}_{\kappa}^{\prime})\cong H^{1}_{c}(\Gamma,\mathcal{D}_{\kappa}^{\prime}).

which allow us to define continuous
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-actions on the groups
H^{1}(\Gamma,\mathcal{A}_{\kappa}^{\prime})
,
H^{1}(\Gamma,\mathcal{D}_{\kappa}^{\prime})
and
H^{1}_{c}(\Gamma,\mathcal{D}_{\kappa}^{\prime})
.

The evaluation map
\mathcal{A}_{\kappa}^{\prime}\otimes_{\Lambda}\mathcal{D}_{\kappa}^{\prime}\rightarrow\Lambda
yields a
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-equivariant cup-product pairing

(5.8)

H^{1}(\Gamma,\mathcal{A}_{\kappa}^{\prime})\otimes_{\Lambda}H^{1}_{c}(\Gamma,\mathcal{D}_{\kappa}^{\prime})\longrightarrow\Lambda(-1)

under which the Hecke operators
T_{q}
,
T_{q}^{\prime}
,
[d]_{N}
,
[d]_{N}^{\prime}
acting covariantly on the left, whenever defined, are adjoint to these same operators acting contravariantly on the right.

Recall that in this section we have set
\Gamma=\Gamma(1,N(pm))
and let
S=\Sigma_{0}^{\prime}(p)\cap\text{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
. For any positive integer
r
, define

\Sigma_{1}^{\prime}(p^{r})=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{Z}_{p}&\mathbb{Z}_{p}\\
p^{r}\mathbb{Z}_{p}&1+p^{r}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\end{pmatrix},\quad S_{r}=\Sigma_{1}^{\prime}(p^{r})\cap\text{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}),\quad\Gamma_{r}=\Gamma(1,Np^{r}(m)).

We define compatibility of Hecke pairs as in
[
AS86a
, Def. 1.1.2]
, but changing left-right conventions. More precisely, we say that the Hecke pair
(\Gamma_{\alpha},S_{\alpha})
is compatible to the Hecke pair
(\Gamma_{\beta},S_{\beta})
if
(\Gamma_{\alpha},S_{\alpha})\subseteq(\Gamma_{\beta},S_{\beta})
,
S_{\alpha}\Gamma_{\beta}=S_{\beta}
and
\Gamma_{\beta}\cap S_{\alpha}^{-1}S_{\alpha}=\Gamma_{\alpha}
. With this definition, the Hecke pair
(\Gamma_{r},S_{r})
is compatible to the Hecke pair
(\Gamma_{t},S_{t})
, if
r\geq t
, and to the Hecke pair
(\Gamma,S)
.

Suppose that the Hecke pair
(\Gamma_{\alpha},S_{\alpha})
is compatible to
(\Gamma_{\beta},S_{\beta})
and that
\Gamma_{\alpha}
has finite index in
\Gamma_{\beta}
. For any right
S_{\alpha}
-module
E
, we define

\Ind_{\Gamma_{\alpha}}^{\Gamma_{\beta}}E=\big\{\varphi:\Gamma_{\beta}\rightarrow E\;|\;\varphi(xy)=\varphi(y)x^{-1}\text{ for all }x\in\Gamma_{\alpha},\,y\in\Gamma_{\beta}\big\}

This module is equipped with a right action of
S_{\beta}
: given
\varphi\in\Ind_{\Gamma_{\alpha}}^{\Gamma_{\beta}}E
and
g\in S_{\beta}

(\varphi g)(x)=\sum\varphi(\gamma)\gamma gx^{-1},

where the sum is over representatives
\gamma
for the cosets in
\Gamma_{\alpha}\backslash\Gamma\cap S_{\alpha}xg^{-1}
.

Now define

\displaystyle A_{\kappa,r}^{\prime}=\big\{f:\Gamma_{1}(p^{r}\mathbb{Z}_{p})\backslash\Gamma_{0}(p\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rightarrow\Lambda_{r}\;|\;f(a\cdot\gamma)=\kappa(a)\cdot f(\gamma)

\displaystyle\text{ for all }a\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times},\,\gamma\in\Gamma_{1}(p^{r}\mathbb{Z}_{p})\backslash\Gamma_{0}(p\mathbb{Z}_{p})\big\}

and let
D_{\kappa,r}^{\prime}=\Hom_{\Lambda_{r}}(A_{\kappa,r}^{\prime},\Lambda_{r})
. With these definitions
\mathcal{D}_{\kappa}^{\prime}=\varprojlim_{r}D_{\kappa,r}^{\prime}
. Let
S_{r}
act trivially on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
and consider the right
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{1}]
-module
\text{Ind}_{\Gamma_{r}}^{\Gamma_{1}}\,\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. Let
R
be a set of representatives for the cosets in
\Gamma_{r}\backslash\Gamma_{1}
. The map
\Ind_{\Gamma_{r}}^{\Gamma_{1}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\rightarrow D_{\kappa,r}^{\prime}
defined by

\varphi\mapsto\big[f\mapsto\sum_{r\in R}\varphi(r)f(r)\big]

is an isomorphism of right
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{1}]
-modules. Therefore, there are natural isomorphisms

H^{1}(\Gamma_{1},\mathcal{D}_{\kappa}^{\prime})\cong\varprojlim_{r}H^{1}(\Gamma_{1},D_{\kappa,r}^{\prime})\cong\varprojlim_{r}H^{1}(\Gamma_{r},\mathbb{Z}_{p}).

According to
[
AS86a
, Lem. 1.1.3]
and
[
AS86a
, Lem. 1.1.4]
, both corestriction and the Shapiro isomorphism commute with the action of
D(\Gamma,S)
via restriction of Hecke algebras, so the previous isomorphisms are Hecke-equivariant.

Similarly to (
5.6
), but omitting
m
from the notation, we let
Y_{r}=Y(1,Np^{r}(m))
and put

H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{\infty,{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}},\mathbb{Z}_{p}):=\varprojlim_{r}H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{r,{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}},\mathbb{Z}_{p}),

where the inverse limit is with respect to the maps
\pi_{1\ast}
. Then

H^{1}(\Gamma_{1},\mathcal{D}_{\kappa}^{\prime})\cong\varprojlim_{r}H^{1}(\Gamma_{r},\mathbb{Z}_{p})\cong H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{\infty,{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}},\mathbb{Z}_{p}),

where the last isomorphism is defined by choosing a compatible system of geometric points for the curves
Y_{r}
and suitable compatible bases for the corresponding Tate modules. Under the isomorphisms above, the contravariant operators
T_{q}^{\prime}
,
[d]_{N}^{\prime}
,
[a]_{p}^{\prime}
on the first term correspond to the contravariant operators
T_{q}^{\prime}
,
\langle d;1\rangle^{\prime}
,
\langle 1;a\rangle^{\prime}
defined on the last term via the compatibility of these operators with the pushforward maps
\pi_{1\ast}
.

Also, according to
[
AS86a
, Lem. 1.1.5]
, the restriction map yields a Hecke-equivariant isomorphism

H^{1}(\Gamma,\mathcal{D}_{\kappa}^{\prime})\cong e_{i}H^{1}(\Gamma_{1},\mathcal{D}_{\kappa}^{\prime})

(recall that we have set
\kappa=\kappa_{i}
). Combining this isomorphism with the previous ones, we obtain a Hecke-equivariant isomorphism

H^{1}(\Gamma,\mathcal{D}_{\kappa}^{\prime})\cong e_{i}H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{\infty,{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}},\mathbb{Z}_{p}).

Similarly, using
[
AS86b
, Prop. 4.2]
, one proves that there is a Hecke-equivariant isomorphism

(5.9)

H^{1}_{c}(\Gamma,\mathcal{D}_{\kappa}^{\prime})\cong e_{i}H^{1}_{\et,c}(Y_{\infty,{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}},\mathbb{Z}_{p}).

6.
Proof of the wild norm relations

Assume that
p
splits in
K
as
(p)=\mathfrak{p}\overline{\mathfrak{p}}
and that it does not divide the class number
h_{K}
.

We keep most of the notations from
\lx@sectionsign\ref{subsec:tameeulersystem}
. In particular,
(g,h)
is a pair of newforms of weights
(l,m)
of the same parity, levels
(N_{g},N_{h})
and characters
(\chi_{g},\chi_{h})
, and we assume that the ring of integers
{\mathcal{O}}\subset E=L_{\mathfrak{P}}
contains the Fourier coefficients of
g
and
h
. In addition, we assume that
p
does not divide
N_{g}
nor
N_{h}
and that both
g
and
h
are ordinary at
p
.

We now allow the Grössencharacter
\psi
to have infinity type
(1-k,0)
for any even integer
k\geq 2
, and let
\mathfrak{f}
be the conductor of
\psi
, which we assume to be coprime to
p
. Let
\chi
be the unique Dirichlet character modulo
N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathfrak{f})
such that
\psi((n))=n^{k-1}\chi(n)
for integers
n
coprime to
N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathfrak{f})
.

As in
[
BL18
, §3.2.1]
, we denote by
\psi_{0}
the unique Grössencharacter of infinity type
(-1,0)
, conductor
\mathfrak{p}
and whose associated
p
-adic Galois character factors through
\Gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}
, the Galois group of the unique
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-extension of
K
unramified outside
\mathfrak{p}
. Then we can uniquely write
\psi=\alpha\psi_{0}^{k-1}
, where
\alpha
is a ray class character of conductor dividing
\mathfrak{f}\mathfrak{p}
.
Since
(\mathfrak{f},p)=1
and
k
is even, it easily follows that
\psi
is
non-Eisenstein
and
p
-distinguished
, meaning that

(6.1)

\alpha\psi_{0}|_{\mathcal{O}_{K,\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}}\not\equiv\omega\;({\rm mod}\;{\mathfrak{P}}),

where
\omega
is the Teichmüller character.

Let
\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}
be the continuous
E
-valued character of
K^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}_{K,\text{f}}^{\times}
defined by

\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}(x)=x_{\mathfrak{p}}^{1-k}\psi(x),

where
x_{\mathfrak{p}}
is the projection of the idèle
x
to the component at
\mathfrak{p}
. We will also denote by
\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}
the corresponding character of
G_{K}
obtained via the geometric Artin map. Then
\Ind_{K}^{\mathbb{Q}}E(\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}^{-1})
is the
p
-adic representation attached to
\theta_{\psi}
, and we note that by (
6.1
) the associated residual representation is absolutely irreducible and
p
-distinguished (see
[
LLZ15
, Rmk. 5.1.4]
).

Consider the
q
-expansion

\Theta=\sum_{(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{fp})=1}[\mathfrak{a}]q^{N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathfrak{a})}\in\mathcal{O}[[H_{\mathfrak{fp}^{\infty}}]][[q]],

where
H_{\mathfrak{fp}^{\infty}}
denotes the maximal pro-
p
quotient of the ray class group of
K
of conductor
\mathfrak{fp}^{\infty}
, and
[\mathfrak{a}]
is the image of
\mathfrak{a}
in
H_{\mathfrak{fp}^{\infty}}
under the geometric Artin map. Since we assume that
p
does not divide
h_{K}
, we can factor
H_{\mathfrak{fp}^{\infty}}\cong H_{\mathfrak{f}}\times\Gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Hence, we have

\Theta\in\mathcal{O}[H_{\mathfrak{f}}]\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\mathcal{O}[[\Gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}]][[q]]
, and we can specialize this to

(6.2)

{\mathbf{f}}=\sum_{(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{fp})=1}\alpha([\mathfrak{a}])\psi_{0}([\mathfrak{a}])[\mathfrak{a}]q^{N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathfrak{a})}\in\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}[[q]],

where
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}=\mathcal{O}[[\Gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}]]
. We identify
\Gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}
with
\Gamma=1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p}
via the isomorphism
\Gamma\cong\mathcal{O}_{K,\mathfrak{p}}^{(1)}\rightarrow\Gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}
defined by
u\mapsto\text{art}_{\mathfrak{p}}(u)^{-1}
, where
\text{art}_{\mathfrak{p}}
stands for the geometric local Artin map, and in this way we identify
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}
with
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}}=\Lambda\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{O}
.
We can therefore regard
{\mathbf{f}}
as a primitive Hida family specializing to

{\mathbf{f}}_{k^{\prime}}=\sum_{(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{fp})=1}\alpha([\mathfrak{a}])\psi_{0}([\mathfrak{a}])^{k^{\prime}-1}q^{N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathfrak{a})}\in S_{k^{\prime}}^{\text{ord}}(N_{\psi}p,\chi_{\alpha}\varepsilon_{K}\omega^{1-k^{\prime}})

at the arithmetic point
\nu_{k^{\prime}-2}
, where
N_{\psi}=DN_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathfrak{f})
and
\chi_{\alpha}(n)=\alpha((n))
. Note that
{\mathbf{f}}
has character
\chi=\chi_{\alpha}\omega^{1-k}
and
{\mathbf{f}}_{k}=\theta_{\psi}^{(p)}
is the ordinary
p
-stabilization of
\theta_{\psi}
.

Let
\chi_{\mathbb{Q}}
be the adelic character attached to
\chi
, let
\chi_{K}=\chi_{\mathbb{Q}}\circ N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}
and let
\psi^{\ast}=\chi_{K}^{-1}\psi
. We can define a primitive Hida family
{\mathbf{f}}^{\ast}
attached to the Grössencharacter
\psi^{\ast}
in the same way that we defined the Hida family
{\mathbf{f}}
attached to
\psi
. This is just the Hida family
{\mathbf{f}}\otimes\chi^{-1}
.

We assume that
\chi\varepsilon_{K}\chi_{g}\chi_{h}=1
, i.e., the product of the characters of
\theta_{\psi}
,
g
and
h
is trivial. Similarly to what we did in §
4
, set

(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3})=(k-2,l-2,m-2)
. For every positive integer
m
, let

\tilde{Y}(m)=Y(1,N(pm)),\quad\textrm{where $N={\rm lcm}(N_{\psi},N_{g},N_{h})$,}

and denote by
\tilde{\Gamma}(m)
the corresponding modular group.
Let
\kappa=\kappa_{r_{1}}:\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\rightarrow\Lambda^{\times}
and choose a square root of this character defined by
\kappa^{1/2}(u)=\omega(u)^{(k-2)/2}[\langle u\rangle^{1/2}]
.

We can define classes

\mathbf{Det}_{m}^{{\mathbf{f}}gh}\in H^{0}_{\et}(\tilde{Y}(m),\bm{\mathcal{A}}_{\kappa}^{\prime}\otimes\bm{\mathcal{A}}_{r_{2}}\otimes\bm{\mathcal{A}}_{r_{3}}(-\kappa^{1/2}-\nu_{(r_{2}+r_{3})/2})).

as in
[
BSV22
, §8.1]
, but replacing the Hida families
{\mathbf{g}},{\mathbf{h}}
in their construction by our
g,h
and working with modules of continuous functions instead of modules of locally analytic functions.
Similarly to what is done in
loc. cit.
, and adopting some of the notations there, we define the cohomology classes

\kappa_{m,{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{(1)}=(e_{\text{ord}}^{\prime}\otimes e_{\text{ord}}\otimes e_{\text{ord}})\circ\mathtt{K}\circ\mathtt{HS}\circ d_{\ast}(\mathbf{Det}_{m}^{{\mathbf{f}}gh}),

inside the group

H^{1}\left(\mathbb{Q},H^{1}(\tilde{\Gamma}(m),\mathcal{A}_{\kappa}^{\prime})^{\text{ord}}\hat{\otimes}H^{1}(\tilde{\Gamma}(m),\mathcal{A}_{r_{2}})^{\text{ord}}\hat{\otimes}H^{1}(\tilde{\Gamma}(m),\mathcal{A}_{r_{3}})^{\text{ord}}(\bm{\kappa}^{1/2}+2+(r_{2}+r_{3})/2)\right),

where
\bm{\kappa}^{1/2}=\kappa^{1/2}\circ\epsilon_{\text{cyc}}
;
and, for each squarefree positive integer
n
coprime to
p
and
N
, we define

\kappa_{n,{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{(2)}=\chi\varepsilon_{K}(n)\kappa(n)^{-1}n^{r_{2}}(\id\otimes\id\otimes[n]_{N})(\id\otimes\pi_{1\ast}\otimes\pi_{2\ast})\kappa_{n^{2},{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{(1)}

lying in the group

H^{1}\left(\mathbb{Q},H^{1}(\tilde{\Gamma}(n^{2}),\mathcal{A}_{\kappa}^{\prime})^{\text{ord}}\hat{\otimes}H^{1}(\tilde{\Gamma}(1),\mathcal{A}_{r_{2}})^{\text{ord}}\hat{\otimes}H^{1}(\tilde{\Gamma}(1),\mathcal{A}_{r_{3}})^{\text{ord}}(\bm{\kappa}^{1/2}+2+(r_{2}+r_{3})/2)\right).

Now we can prove norm relations for
\Lambda
-adic classes as we did for the classes in the previous section.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
m
be a positive integer and let
q
be a prime number. Assume that both
m
and
q
are coprime to
p
and
N
. Then

\displaystyle(\pi_{2\ast}\otimes\pi_{1\ast}\otimes\pi_{1\ast})\kappa_{mq,{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{(1)}=(T_{q}^{\prime}\otimes\id\otimes\id)\kappa_{m,{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{(1)};

\displaystyle(\pi_{1\ast}\otimes\pi_{2\ast}\otimes\pi_{2\ast})\kappa_{mq,{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{(1)}=\kappa^{-1/2}(q)q^{(r_{2}+r_{3})/2}(T_{q}\otimes\id\otimes\id)\kappa_{m,{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{(1)};

\displaystyle(\pi_{1\ast}\otimes\pi_{2\ast}\otimes\pi_{1\ast})\kappa_{mq,{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{(1)}=(\id\otimes T_{q}^{\prime}\otimes\id)\kappa_{m,{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{(1)};

\displaystyle(\pi_{2\ast}\otimes\pi_{1\ast}\otimes\pi_{2\ast})\kappa_{mq,{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{(1)}=\kappa^{1/2}(q)q^{(r_{3}-r_{2})/2}(\id\otimes T_{q}\otimes\id)\kappa_{m,{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{(1)};

\displaystyle(\pi_{1\ast}\otimes\pi_{1\ast}\otimes\pi_{2\ast})\kappa_{mq,{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{(1)}=(\id\otimes\id\otimes T_{q}^{\prime})\kappa_{m,{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{(1)};

\displaystyle(\pi_{2\ast}\otimes\pi_{2\ast}\otimes\pi_{1\ast})\kappa_{mq,{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{(1)}=\kappa^{1/2}(q)q^{(r_{2}-r_{3})/2}(\id\otimes\id\otimes T_{q})\kappa_{m,{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{(1)}.

If
q
is coprime to
m
we also have

\displaystyle(\pi_{1\ast}\otimes\pi_{1\ast}\otimes\pi_{1\ast})\kappa_{mq,{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{(1)}=(q+1)\kappa_{m,{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{(1)};

\displaystyle(\pi_{2\ast}\otimes\pi_{2\ast}\otimes\pi_{2\ast})\kappa_{mq,{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{(1)}=(q+1)\kappa^{1/2}(q)q^{(r_{2}+r_{3})/2}\kappa_{m,{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{(1)}.

Proof.

As in Lemma
4.2
, the same arguments proving equations (174) and (176) in
[
BSV22
]
apply
mutatis mutandis
to yield the proof of these identities.
∎

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
n
be a squarefree positive integer coprime to
p
and
N
and let
q
be a rational prime coprime to
p
,
N
and
n
. Then

\displaystyle(\pi_{11\ast}\otimes\id\otimes\id)\kappa_{nq,{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=\big\{\chi(q)\kappa(q)^{-1}q^{r_{2}}(\id\otimes\id\otimes[q]_{N}^{-1}T_{q}^{2})

\displaystyle\quad-\chi(q)\kappa(q)^{-1}(q+1)q^{r_{2}+r_{3}}(\id\otimes\id\otimes\id)\big\}\kappa_{n,{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{(2)},

\displaystyle(\pi_{21\ast}\otimes\id\otimes\id)\kappa_{nq,{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=\big\{\chi(q)\kappa^{-1/2}(q)q^{(r_{2}+r_{3})/2}(\id\otimes T_{q}\otimes T_{q})

\displaystyle\quad-\chi(q)\kappa(q)^{-1}q^{r_{2}+r_{3}}(([q]_{N}^{\prime})^{-1}T_{q}^{\prime}\otimes[q]_{N}\otimes[q]_{N})\big\}\kappa_{n,{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{(2)},

\displaystyle(\pi_{22\ast}\otimes\id\otimes\id)\kappa_{nq,{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=\big\{\chi(q)q^{r_{3}}(\id\otimes T_{q}^{2}\otimes[q]_{N})

\displaystyle\quad-\chi(q)(q+1)q^{r_{2}+r_{3}}(\id\otimes[q]_{N}\otimes[q]_{N})\big\}\kappa_{n,{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{(2)},

where
\pi_{ij*}
denotes the composition

H^{1}(\tilde{\Gamma}(n^{2}q^{2}),\mathcal{F})\overset{\pi_{i*}}{\longrightarrow}H^{1}(\tilde{\Gamma}(n^{2}q),\mathcal{F})\overset{\pi_{j*}}{\longrightarrow}H^{1}(\tilde{\Gamma}(n^{2}),\mathcal{F}).

Proof.

This can be deduced from Lemma
6.1
by the same calculation as in Lemma
4.4
.
∎

Let
\Gamma(m)=\Gamma(1,Np(m))
and write
Y(m)
and
X(m)
for the corresponding affine and projective modular curves. The pairing in equation (
5.8
)
yields a map

H^{1}(\tilde{\Gamma}(m),\mathcal{A}_{\kappa}^{\prime})\rightarrow\Hom_{\Lambda}(H^{1}_{c}(\Gamma(m),\mathcal{D}_{\kappa}^{\prime}),\Lambda)(-1)\cong\Hom_{\Lambda}(e_{r_{1}}H^{1}_{\et,c}(Y_{\infty}(m)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathbb{Z}_{p}),\Lambda)(-1),

where the isomorphism comes from equation (
5.9
). Let
\mathcal{I}_{n}
be the maximal ideal in Hida’s big ordinary Hecke algebra
\mathbb{T}(1,Np^{\infty}(n^{2}))^{\prime}_{\text{ord}}
corresponding to the Hida family
{\mathbf{f}}^{\ast}
; by (
6.1
) this ideal corresponds to a non-Eisenstein maximal ideal in
\mathbb{T}(1,Np(n^{2}))^{\prime}
, so there are isomorphisms

H^{1}_{\et,c}(Y_{\infty}(n^{2})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\text{ord}}_{\mathcal{I}_{n}}\cong H^{1}_{\et}(X_{\infty}(n^{2})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\text{ord}}_{\mathcal{I}_{n}}\cong H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{\infty}(n^{2})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\text{ord}}_{\mathcal{I}_{n}}.

Hence, the pairings (
5.8
) and (
5.7
) together with the isomorphism (
5.9
) yield a morphism

\mathtt{s}_{{\mathbf{f}},n\ast}:H^{1}(\tilde{\Gamma}(n^{2}),\mathcal{A}_{\kappa}^{\prime})^{\text{ord}}\longrightarrow e_{r_{1}}H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{\infty}(n^{2})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\text{ord}}_{\mathcal{I}_{n}}(\langle\epsilon_{N}^{-1};1\rangle^{\prime})(-\bm{\kappa}).

This map is
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-equivariant and intertwines the covariant action of the operators
T_{q}^{\prime}
,
[d]_{N}^{\prime}
,
[a]_{p}^{\prime}
on the source with the contravariant action of the operators
T_{q}^{\prime}
,
\langle d;1\rangle^{\prime}
,
\langle 1;a\rangle^{\prime}
on the target.

Fix a level-
N
test vector
\breve{{\mathbf{f}}}
for
{\mathbf{f}}
and let
\breve{{\mathbf{f}}}^{\ast}=\breve{{\mathbf{f}}}\otimes\chi^{-1}\varepsilon_{K}
. Fix also test vectors

\breve{g}\in S_{l}(N,\chi_{g})[g],\quad\breve{h}\in S_{m}(N,\chi_{h})[h]

and write
\breve{g}_{\alpha}
and
\breve{h}_{\alpha}
for the corresponding ordinary
p
-stabilizations.

Define maps

\phi_{n,r}:\mathbb{T}(1,N_{\psi}p^{r}(n^{2}))_{\text{ord}}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}[R_{\overline{\mathfrak{f}}\mathfrak{p}^{r},n}]

attached to the Grössencharacter
\alpha\chi_{K}^{-1}\psi_{0}
as in Lemma
3.5
and let

\phi_{n,\infty}:\mathbb{T}(1,N_{\psi}p^{\infty}(n^{2}))_{\text{ord}}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}[[R_{\overline{\mathfrak{f}}\mathfrak{p}^{\infty},n}]]=\mathcal{O}[R_{\overline{\mathfrak{f}},n}]\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\mathcal{O}[[\Gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}]].

be the inverse limit
\varprojlim_{r}\phi_{n,r}
. The test vector
\breve{{\mathbf{f}}}^{\ast}
determines a degeneracy map

H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{\infty}(n^{2})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1))^{\text{ord}}\rightarrow H^{1}_{\et}(Y(1,N_{\psi}p^{\infty}(n^{2}))_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1))^{\text{ord}}.

Composing this degeneracy map with the natural quotient map we get a morphism

\pi_{{\mathbf{f}}^{\ast}}:e_{r_{1}}H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{\infty}(n^{2})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathcal{O}(1))^{\text{ord}}_{\mathcal{I}_{n}}\rightarrow(\mathcal{O}[R_{n}]\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\mathcal{O}[[\Gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}]])\otimes_{\phi_{n,\infty}}H^{1}_{\et}(Y(1,N_{\psi}p^{\infty}(n^{2}))_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathcal{O}(1))^{\text{ord}}.

The test vectors
\breve{g}_{\alpha}
and
\breve{h}_{\alpha}
determine degeneracy maps

\displaystyle H^{1}_{\et}(\tilde{Y}(1)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathscr{L}_{r_{2}}(1))\xrightarrow{\mu_{p}^{\ast}}H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{1}(Np)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathscr{L}_{r_{2}}(1))

\displaystyle\rightarrow H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{1}(N_{g})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathscr{L}_{r_{2}}(1))

\displaystyle H^{1}_{\et}(\tilde{Y}(1)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathscr{L}_{r_{3}}(1))\xrightarrow{\mu_{p}^{\ast}}H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{1}(Np)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathscr{L}_{r_{3}}(1))

\displaystyle\rightarrow H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{1}(N_{h})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathscr{L}_{r_{3}}(1)).

Composing these maps with projection to the
g
-isotypic and
h
-isotypic quotient, respectively, we obtain

\displaystyle\pi_{g}:e_{\text{ord}}^{\prime}H^{1}(\tilde{\Gamma}(1),L_{r_{2}}(1))\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{O}\longrightarrow T_{g}

\displaystyle\pi_{h}:e_{\text{ord}}^{\prime}H^{1}(\tilde{\Gamma}(1),L_{r_{3}}(1))\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{O}\longrightarrow T_{h}.

For the ease of notation, we write

H^{1}(\psi,\overline{\mathfrak{f}},n)=(\mathcal{O}[R_{\overline{\mathfrak{f}},n}]\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\mathcal{O}[[\Gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}]])\otimes_{\phi_{n,\infty}}H^{1}_{\et}(Y(1,N_{\psi}p^{\infty}(n^{2}))_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathcal{O})^{\text{ord}}(\langle\epsilon_{N}^{-1};1\rangle^{\prime})(\bm{\kappa}^{-1/2})

and put
H^{1}(\psi,n)=\mathcal{O}[R_{n}]\otimes_{\mathcal{O}[R_{\overline{\mathfrak{f}},n}]}H^{1}(\psi,\overline{\mathfrak{f}},n)
. Then we define the class

(6.3)

\kappa_{n,{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{(3)}=(\pi_{{\mathbf{f}}^{\ast}}\otimes\pi_{g}\otimes\pi_{h})\circ(\mathtt{s}_{{\mathbf{f}}\ast}\otimes\mathtt{s}_{r_{2}\ast}\otimes\mathtt{s}_{r_{3}\ast})\kappa_{n,{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{(2)}\\

lying in the group

H^{1}\left(\mathbb{Q},H^{1}(\psi,n)\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}(T_{g}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}T_{h})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}(-1-(r_{2}+r_{3})/2)\right).

Let
\Gamma_{\text{ac}}
be the Galois group of the anticyclotomic
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-extension of
K
. We can identify this group with the anti-diagonal in
(1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p})\times(1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p})\cong\mathcal{O}_{K,\mathfrak{p}}^{(1)}\times\mathcal{O}_{K,\overline{\mathfrak{p}}}^{(1)}
via the geometrically normalized Artin map. Let
\kappa_{\text{ac}}:\Gamma_{\text{ac}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
be the character defined by mapping
((1+p)^{-1/2},(1+p)^{1/2})
to
1+p
and let
\bm{\kappa}_{\text{ac}}:\Gamma_{\text{ac}}\rightarrow\Lambda^{\times}
be the character defined by mapping
((1+p)^{-1/2},(1+p)^{1/2})
to the group-like element
[1+p]
. We use the same notation for the corresponding characters of
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
.
There is a
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-equivariant isomorphism of
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}}[R_{n}]
-modules

(6.4)

H^{1}(\psi,n)\cong\Ind_{K[n]}^{\mathbb{Q}}\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}}(\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}^{-1}\kappa_{\text{ac}}^{r_{1}/2}\bm{\kappa}_{\text{ac}}^{-1})(-r_{1}/2).

Let

T_{g,h}^{\psi}=T_{g}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}T_{h}(\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}^{-1})(-1-r),\quad V_{g,h}^{\psi}=T_{g,h}^{\psi}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}.

In light of the isomorphism (
6.4
), using Shapiro’s lemma
the classes
\kappa_{n,{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{(3)}
yield classes

(6.5)

\tilde{\kappa}_{\psi,g,h,n,\infty}\in H^{1}(K[n],\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}}(\bm{\kappa}_{\text{ac}}^{-1})\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}T_{g,h}^{\psi}(\kappa_{\text{ac}}^{r_{1}/2}))\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}E

for every squarefree integer
n
coprime to
p
and
N
.

Proposition 6.3
.

Let
n
be as above and let
q
be a rational prime coprime to
p
,
N
and
n
. Then:

(i)

If
q
splits in
K
as
(q)=\mathfrak{q}\overline{\mathfrak{q}}
,

\displaystyle\cor_{K[nq]/K[n]}(\tilde{\kappa}_{\psi,g,h,nq,\infty})

\displaystyle=q^{l+m-4}\Bigg\{\chi_{g}(q)\chi_{h}(q)q\left(\frac{\kappa_{\ac}^{-(k-2)/2}\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}(\mathrm{Fr}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-1})}{q^{k-1}}\mathrm{Fr}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-1}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{a_{q}(g)a_{q}(h)}{q^{(l+m-4)/2}}\left(\frac{\kappa_{\ac}^{-(k-2)/2}\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}(\mathrm{Fr}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-1})}{q^{k-1}}\mathrm{Fr}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{\chi_{g}(q)^{-1}a_{q}(g)^{2}}{q^{l-2}}+\frac{\chi_{h}(q)^{-1}a_{q}(h)^{2}}{q^{m-1}}-\frac{q^{2}+1}{q}

\displaystyle-\frac{a_{q}(g)a_{q}(h)}{q^{(l+m-4)/2}}\left(\frac{\kappa_{\ac}^{-(k-2)/2}\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}(\mathrm{Fr}_{\overline{\mathfrak{q}}}^{-1})}{q^{k-1}}\mathrm{Fr}_{\overline{\mathfrak{q}}}^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle+\chi_{g}(q)\chi_{h}(q)q\left(\frac{\kappa_{\ac}^{-(k-2)/2}\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}(\mathrm{Fr}_{\overline{\mathfrak{q}}}^{-1})}{q^{k-1}}\mathrm{Fr}_{\overline{\mathfrak{q}}}^{-1}\right)^{2}\Bigg\}\tilde{\kappa}_{\psi,g,h,n,\infty}.

(ii)

If
q
is inert in
K
,

\displaystyle\cor_{K[nq]/K[n]}(\tilde{\kappa}_{\psi,g,h,nq,\infty})

\displaystyle=q^{l+m-4}\Bigg\{\frac{\chi_{g}(q)^{-1}a_{q}(g)^{2}}{q^{l-2}}+\frac{\chi_{h}(q)^{-1}a_{q}(h)^{2}}{q^{m-1}}-\frac{(q+1)^{2}}{q}\Bigg\}\tilde{\kappa}_{\psi,g,h,n,\infty}.

Proof.

The proof of this proposition is similar to the proof of Proposition
4.5
. We just remark that the maps
\mathtt{s}_{{\mathbf{f}},n\ast}
interchange the degeneracy maps
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
, and under the isomorphism

\displaystyle H^{1}(K[n],\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}}(\bm{\kappa}_{\text{ac}}^{-1})

\displaystyle\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}T_{g,h}^{\psi}(\kappa_{\ac}^{(k-2)/2}))\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}E

\displaystyle\cong H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},H^{1}(\psi,n)\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}(T_{g}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}T_{h})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}(-1-(r_{2}+r_{3})/2))

arising from (
6.4
), the corestriction
{\rm cor}_{K[nq]/K[n]}
corresponds, in the case where
(q)=\mathfrak{q}\overline{\mathfrak{q}}
splits in
K
, to the map

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{n}^{nq}

\displaystyle=\pi_{11\ast}-\chi^{-1}(q)\omega^{(k-2)/2}(q)\left(\frac{\kappa_{\ac}^{-(k-2)/2}\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}({\rm Fr}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-1})[\mathfrak{q}]}{q^{k/2}}+\frac{\kappa_{\ac}^{-(k-2)/2}\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}({\rm Fr}_{\overline{\mathfrak{q}}}^{-1})[\overline{\mathfrak{q}}]}{q^{k/2}}\right)\pi_{21\ast}

\displaystyle+\frac{\chi^{-1}(q)\omega^{k-2}(q)}{q}\pi_{22\ast},

and similarly in the case where
q
is inert in
K
. Since the result can be deduced from Lemma
6.1
by virtually the same calculation as in the proof of Lemma
4.4
, we omit the details.
∎

Definition 6.4
.

For any
E
-valued
G_{K}
-representation
V
, put

H^{1}_{\text{Iw}}(K[np^{\infty}],T):=\varprojlim_{r}H^{1}(K[np^{r}],T),\quad H^{1}_{\text{Iw}}(K[np^{\infty}],V):=H^{1}_{\text{Iw}}(K[np^{\infty}],T)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}E,

where
T\subset V
is a Galois stable
\mathcal{O}
-lattice.

By another application of Shapiro’s lemma, the classes
{\kappa_{\psi,g,h,n,\infty}}
in (
6.5
) naturally live in
H^{1}_{\text{Iw}}(K[np^{\infty}],V_{g,h}^{\psi}(\kappa_{\text{ac}}^{(k-2)/2}))
.
We thus arrive at the following theorem, which is the main result of this section.

Theorem 6.5
.

Suppose that:

•

l\geq m\geq 2
have the same parity and
k\geq 2
is even,

•

p
splits in
K
,

•

p
does not divide the class number of
K
.

Let
\mathcal{S}
be the set of squarefree products of primes
q
which split in
K
and are coprime to
p
and
N
. Assume that
H^{1}(K[np^{s}],T_{g,h}^{\psi})
is torsion-free for every
n\in\mathcal{S}
and for every
s\geq 0
. There exists a collection of classes

\left\{{\kappa_{\psi,g,h,n,\infty}}\in H^{1}_{\rm Iw}(K[np^{\infty}],T_{g,h}^{\psi})\;\colon\;n\in\mathcal{S}\right\}

such that whenever
n,nq\in\mathcal{S}
with
q
a prime, we have

{\rm cor}_{K[nq]/K[n]}({\kappa_{\psi,g,h,nq,\infty}})=P_{\mathfrak{q}}({\rm Fr}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-1})\,{\kappa_{\psi,g,h,n,\infty}},

where
\mathfrak{q}
is any of the primes of
K
above
q
, and
P_{\mathfrak{q}}(X)=\det(1-{\rm Fr}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-1}X|(V_{g,h}^{\psi})^{\vee}(1))
.

Proof.

The same argument as in the proof of Theorem
4.6
(but using Proposition
6.3
) yields a system of Iwasawa cohomology classes with the stated norm-compatibilities for the representation
V_{g,h}^{\psi}(\kappa_{\text{ac}}^{(k-2)/2})
. By the twisting result of
[
Rub00
, Thm. 6.3.5]
, the theorem follows.
∎

We conclude this section by proving that
the classes
{\kappa_{\psi,g,h,n,\infty}}
land in the
balanced Selmer group

\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(K[np^{\infty}],T_{g,h}^{\psi}):=\varprojlim_{r}\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(K[np^{r}],T_{g,h}^{\psi});

in the terminology introduced in
\lx@sectionsign\ref{sec:ES}
below,
this is the same as the Greenberg Selmer group
{\rm Sel}_{\rm Gr}(K[np^{\infty}],T_{g,h}^{\psi})
associated to the
G_{K_{v}}
-invariant subspaces
\mathcal{F}_{v}^{+}(V_{g,h}^{\psi})\subset V_{g,h}^{\psi}
in (
4.1
)
at the primes
v\mid p
.

Proposition 6.6
.

For all
n\in\mathcal{S}
, we have
{\kappa_{\psi,g,h,n,\infty}}\in\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(K[np^{\infty}],T_{g,h}^{\psi})
.

Proof.

Let
v\nmid p
be a finite prime of
K[np^{\infty}]
, and for every
r\geq 0
denote also by
v
the prime of
K[np^{r}]
below
v
. As in the proof of Proposition
4.9
, we have

H^{1}(K[np^{r}]_{v},V_{g,h}^{\psi})=H^{1}_{\rm Gr}(K[np^{r}]_{v},V_{g,h}^{\psi})=0,

and hence

H^{1}(K[np^{r}]_{v},T_{g,h}^{\psi})=H^{1}(K[np^{r}]_{v},T_{g,h}^{\psi})_{\rm tors}=H^{1}_{\rm Gr}(K[np^{r}]_{v},T_{g,h}^{\psi}),

where the first equality follows from the local Euler characteristic formula. Hence the inclusion
{\rm res}_{v}({\kappa_{\psi,g,h,n,\infty}})\in\varprojlim_{r}H^{1}_{\rm Gr}(K[np^{r}]_{v},T^{\psi}_{g,h})
follows. Since by
[
BSV22
, Cor. 8.2]
it follows that the classes
{\kappa_{\psi,g,h,n,\infty}}
satisfy the balanced local condition at the primes above
p
, this concludes the proof.
∎

Part II
Arithmetic applications

7.
Iwasawa main conjectures

In this section we formulate Iwasawa main conjectures for triple products of modular forms. We give two formulations: one in terms of the triple product
p
-adic
L
-function (Conjecture
7.7
) and another in terms of diagonal cycle classes (Conjecture
7.9
). In Theorem
7.15
we establish the equivalence of the two formulations.

7.1.
Triple product
p
-adic
L
-function

Fix a triple
({\mathbf{f}},g,h)
consisting of a primitive Hida family
{\mathbf{f}}
of tame level
N_{{\mathbf{f}}}
and character
\chi_{{\mathbf{f}}}
and two
p
-ordinary newforms
g,h
of weights
l,m\geq 2
, levels
N_{g},N_{h}
prime-to-
p
, and nebentypus
\chi_{g},\chi_{h}
. Assume that
{\mathbf{f}}
has coefficients in a ring
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}
as in §
5.1
. Assume that
\chi_{{\mathbf{f}}}\chi_{g}\chi_{h}=\omega^{r_{1}}
for some even integer
r_{1}
and put

N={\rm lcm}(N_{{\mathbf{f}}},N_{g},N_{h}).

Let
{\mathbf{g}}
and
{\mathbf{h}}
be primitive Hida families with coefficients in
\Lambda_{\mathbf{g}}
and
\Lambda_{\mathbf{h}}
passing through
g
and
h
, respectively. More precisely, there exist arithmetic points
y_{0}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
and
z_{0}\in\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
such that
g_{y_{0}}
and
h_{z_{0}}
are the ordinary
p
-stabilizations of
g
and
h
, respectively. The rings
\Lambda_{\mathbf{g}}
and
\Lambda_{\mathbf{h}}
need not be regular. However, for our purposes, we can consider the
\Lambda
-adic families, denoted again
{\mathbf{g}}
and
{\mathbf{h}}
, that result from embedding
\Lambda_{\mathbf{g}}
and
\Lambda_{\mathbf{h}}
in the rings of functions of suitable wide open connected subsets
U_{\mathbf{g}}
and
U_{\mathbf{h}}
of
\mathcal{W}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})=\mathrm{Spf}(\Lambda)(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
defined over some finite extension
E
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and containing the points
y_{0}
and
z_{0}
, respectively. From now on, it is these rings of functions that we will denote by
\Lambda_{\mathbf{g}}
and
\Lambda_{\mathbf{h}}
. These rings are now non-canonically isomorphic to
{\mathcal{O}}[[T]]
, where
\mathcal{O}
is the ring of integers of
E
; in particular, they are regular. Let
\bm{l}-l
and
\bm{m}-m
be generators in
\Lambda_{\mathbf{g}}
and
\Lambda_{\mathbf{h}}
of the prime ideals corresponding to the points
y_{0}
and
z_{0}
, respectively.

We can and will assume that
\Lambda_{\mathbf{f}}
is a finite flat extension of
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}}
and we will only consider arithmetic points in
\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda_{\mathbf{f}}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
lying in
\Hom_{\text{cont},\mathcal{O}}(\Lambda_{\mathbf{f}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})

Recall that in
\lx@sectionsign\ref{subsubsec:Lambda-pairing}
we defined a character
\kappa_{r_{1}}:\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\rightarrow\Lambda^{\times}
given by
u\mapsto\omega^{r_{1}}(u)[\langle u\rangle]
and in
\lx@sectionsign\ref{subsec:Iw}
we fixed a square root
\kappa_{r_{1}}^{1/2}
of this character given by
u\mapsto\omega^{r_{1}/2}(u)[\langle u\rangle^{1/2}]
. We let
\kappa_{\mathbf{f}}
and
\kappa_{\mathbf{f}}^{1/2}
be the composition of
\kappa_{r_{1}}
and
\kappa_{r_{1}}^{1/2}
, respectively, with the embedding
\Lambda^{\times}\xhookrightarrow{}\Lambda_{\mathbf{f}}^{\times}
. We also define a character
\kappa_{{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}:\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\rightarrow(\Lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\Lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}})^{\times}
by

\kappa_{{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}(u)=\omega(u)^{l+m-4}\langle u\rangle^{\bm{l}+{\mathbf{m}}-4}

and choose a square-root of this character defined by

\kappa_{{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}^{1/2}(u)=\omega(u)^{(l+m-4)/2}\langle u\rangle^{(\bm{l}+{\mathbf{m}}-4)/2}
. Let
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}=\Lambda_{\mathbf{f}}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\Lambda_{\mathbf{g}}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\Lambda_{\mathbf{h}}
and consider the
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}[G_{\mathbb{Q}}]
-module

(7.1)

\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}^{\dagger}:=\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{g}}}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{h}}}(\Xi_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}),\quad\;\textrm{where\; $\Xi_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}=\epsilon_{\rm cyc}^{-1}\kappa_{{\mathbf{f}}}^{-1/2}\kappa_{{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}^{-1/2}$}

and
\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}}
,
\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{g}}}
and
\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{h}}}
are the big Galois representations attached to
{\mathbf{f}}
,
{\mathbf{g}}
and
{\mathbf{h}}
, respectively. Then
\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}^{\dagger}
is a self-dual twist of the tensor product of these representations. Consider also the
\Lambda_{\mathbf{f}}[G_{\mathbb{Q}}]
-module

\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger}:=\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}T_{g}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}T_{h}(\Xi_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}),\quad\;\textrm{where\; $\Xi_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}=\epsilon_{\rm cyc}^{(2-l-m)/2}\kappa_{{\mathbf{f}}}^{-1/2}$.}

Given test vectors
(\breve{{\mathbf{f}}},\breve{g},\breve{h})
for
({\mathbf{f}},g,h)
of level
N
, as explained in
[
HT01
]
and
[
DR14
, §4.2]
, a generalization of Hida’s
p
-adic Rankin–Selberg convolution produces an element
\mathscr{L}_{p}(\breve{{\mathbf{f}}},\breve{g},\breve{h})
in the fraction field of
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}
whose specializations to arithmetic points
x\in\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda_{\mathbf{f}}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
of even weight
k\geq l+m

recover (a square-root of) the central critical values of the triple product
L
-function

L(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}_{x}gh}^{\dagger},s)
for the specialization of
\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger}
at
x
by virtue of Harris–Kudla’s proof of Jacquet’s conjecture,
[
HK91
]
. A recent result by Hsieh
[
Hsi21
]
constructs test vectors
(\underline{\breve{{\mathbf{f}}}},\underline{\breve{g}},\underline{\breve{h}})
for which a precise interpolation property for the resulting
\mathscr{L}_{p}(\underline{\breve{{\mathbf{f}}}},\underline{\breve{g}},\underline{\breve{h}})
is proved. To recall the result in the form that will be used here, for any arithmetic point
x\in\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda_{\mathbf{f}}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
as above, we set

{\mathbf{f}}_{k}:={\mathbf{f}}_{x},\quad\alpha_{k}:=a_{p}({\mathbf{f}}_{k}),\quad\beta_{k}:=\chi_{{\mathbf{f}}}(p)p^{k-1}\alpha_{k}^{-1},

let
\alpha_{g},\beta_{g}
be the roots of the Hecke polynomial of
g
at
p
with
{\rm ord}_{p}(\alpha_{g})=0
, and define
\alpha_{h},\beta_{h}
similarly. As recalled in [
op. cit.
, §1.4], when the residual Galois representation
\bar{\rho}_{{\mathbf{f}}}
associated to
{\mathbf{f}}
is absolutely irreducible and
p
-distinguished, the local ring
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}
is known to be Gorenstein and by a result of Hida’s the congruence module of
{\mathbf{f}}
is isomorphic to
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}/(\xi)
for some nonzero
\xi\in\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}
. We call
(\xi)
the
congruence ideal
of
{\mathbf{f}}
. Finally, denote by

\varepsilon_{\ell}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}_{k}gh}^{\dagger})\in\{\pm{1}\}
the epsilon factor of the Weil–Deligne representation attached to the restriction of
\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}_{k}gh}^{\dagger}
to
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}}
.

Theorem 7.1
.

In addition to
\chi_{{\mathbf{f}}}\chi_{g}\chi_{h}=\omega^{r_{1}}
, assume that:

(a)

\bar{\rho}_{{\mathbf{f}}}
is absolutely irreducible and
p
-distinguished,

(b)

for some arithmetic point
x\in\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda_{\mathbf{f}}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
, we have
\varepsilon_{\ell}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}_{k}gh}^{\dagger})=+1
for all primes
\ell\mid N
,

(c)

{\rm gcd}(N_{{\mathbf{f}}},N_{g},N_{h})
is squarefree.

Let
\xi
be a generator of the congruence ideal of
{\mathbf{f}}
. There exist test vectors
(\underline{\breve{{\mathbf{f}}}},\underline{\breve{g}},\underline{\breve{h}})
for
({\mathbf{f}},g,h)
of level
N
, and an element

\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\xi}(\underline{\breve{{\mathbf{f}}}},\underline{\breve{g}},\underline{\breve{h}})\in\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}

such that for all arithmetic points
x\in\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda_{\mathbf{f}}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
of even weight
k\geq l+m
with
k\equiv r_{1}+2\pmod{2(p-1)}

we have

\biggl(\frac{\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\xi}(\underline{\breve{{\mathbf{f}}}},\underline{\breve{g}},\underline{\breve{h}})(x)}{\xi_{x}}\biggr)^{2}=\frac{\Gamma(k,l,m)}{2^{\alpha(k,l,m)}}\cdot\frac{\mathcal{E}({\mathbf{f}}_{k},g,h)^{2}}{\mathcal{E}_{0}({\mathbf{f}}_{k})^{2}\cdot\mathcal{E}_{1}({\mathbf{f}}_{k})^{2}}\cdot\prod_{\ell\mid N}\tau_{\ell}\cdot\frac{L(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}_{k}gh}^{\dagger},0)}{\pi^{2k}\cdot\langle{\mathbf{f}}_{k}^{\sharp},{\mathbf{f}}_{k}^{\sharp}\rangle^{2}},

where:

•

\Gamma(k,l,m)=(c-1)!\cdot(c-m)!\cdot(c-l)!\cdot(c+1-l-m)!
, with
c=(k+l+m-2)/2
,

•

\alpha(k,l,m)\in\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}
is a linear form in the variables
k
,
l
,
m
,

•

\mathcal{E}({\mathbf{f}}_{k},g,h)=(1-\frac{\beta_{k}\alpha_{g}\alpha_{h}}{p^{c}})(1-\frac{\beta_{k}\beta_{g}\alpha_{h}}{p^{c}})(1-\frac{\beta_{k}\alpha_{g}\beta_{h}}{p^{c}})(1-\frac{\beta_{k}\beta_{g}\beta_{h}}{p^{c}})
,

•

\mathcal{E}_{0}({\mathbf{f}}_{k})=(1-\frac{\beta_{k}}{\alpha_{k}})
,

\mathcal{E}_{1}({\mathbf{f}}_{k})=(1-\frac{\beta_{k}}{p\alpha_{k}})
,

•

\tau_{\ell}
is an explicit nonzero rational number independent of
k
,

•

{\mathbf{f}}_{k}^{\sharp}
is the newform associated to the
p
-stabilized newform
{\mathbf{f}}_{k}
,

and

\lVert{\mathbf{f}}_{k}^{\sharp}\rVert^{2}
is the Petersson norm of
{\mathbf{f}}_{k}^{\sharp}
.

Proof.

Letting
{\mathbf{g}},{\mathbf{h}}
be the primitive Hida families of tame level
N_{g},N_{h}
passing through the ordinary
p
-stabilizations of
g,h
, this follows by specializing the three-variable
p
-adic
L
-function in
[
Hsi21
, Thm. A]
attached to
({{\mathbf{f}}},{{\mathbf{g}}},{{\mathbf{h}}})

and the congruence ideal generator
\xi
.
∎

Definition 7.2
.

For the test vectors
(\underline{\breve{{\mathbf{f}}}},\underline{\breve{g}},\underline{\breve{h}})
of level
N
provided by Theorem
7.1
, we set

L_{p}({\mathbf{f}},g,h):=\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\xi}(\underline{\breve{{\mathbf{f}}}},\underline{\breve{g}},\underline{\breve{h}})^{2},

where
\xi
is any fixed generator of the congruence ideal of
{\mathbf{f}}
.

Note that
L_{p}({\mathbf{f}},g,h)
depends on the choice of
\xi
, but the principal ideal in
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}
it generates is of course independent of that choice.

7.2.
Reciprocity law for diagonal cycles

Keep the notations in the previous subsection and without loss of generality assume that
l\geq m
(reordering
g
and
h
if necessary).

Assume that the Galois representations attached to
{\mathbf{f}}
,
g
and
h
are all residually irreducible and
p
-distinguished. Let
\bm{\phi}\in\{{\mathbf{f}},{\mathbf{g}},{\mathbf{h}}\}
. As a
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-representation,
\mathbb{V}_{\bm{\phi}}
admits a filtration

(7.2)

0\rightarrow\mathbb{V}_{\bm{\phi}}^{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{V}_{\bm{\phi}}\rightarrow\mathbb{V}_{\bm{\phi}}^{-}\rightarrow 0

with each
\mathbb{V}_{\bm{\phi}}^{\pm}
free of rank one over
\Lambda_{\bm{\phi}}
, and with the
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-action on
\mathbb{V}_{\bm{\phi}}^{-}
given by the unramified character sending
{\rm Fr}_{p}\mapsto a_{p}(\bm{\phi})
.
This induces an obvious three-step filtration

0\subset\mathscr{F}^{3}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}^{{\dagger}}\subset\mathscr{F}^{2}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}^{\dagger}\subset\mathscr{F}^{1}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}^{\dagger}\subset\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}^{\dagger}

by
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-stable
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}
-submodules of ranks 1, 4, and 7, respectively, given by

(7.3)

\begin{split}\mathscr{F}^{1}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}^{\dagger}&=(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{g}}}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{h}}}^{+}+\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{g}}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{h}}}+\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{g}}}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{h}}})(\Xi_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}),\\
\mathscr{F}^{2}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}^{\dagger}&=(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{g}}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{h}}}^{+}+\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{g}}}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{h}}}^{+}+\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{g}}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{h}}})(\Xi_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}),\\
\mathscr{F}^{3}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}^{{\dagger}}&=\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{g}}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{h}}}^{+}(\Xi_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}).\end{split}

The middle term
\mathscr{F}^{2}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}^{\dagger}
will play a special role in the following, and we note that

(7.4)

\mathscr{F}^{2}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}^{\dagger}/\mathscr{F}^{3}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}^{{\dagger}}\cong\mathbb{V}_{\mathbf{f}}^{{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}\oplus\mathbb{V}_{\mathbf{g}}^{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{h}}}\oplus\mathbb{V}_{\mathbf{h}}^{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}},

where
\mathbb{V}_{\mathbf{f}}^{{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}:=(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}}^{-}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{g}}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{h}}}^{+})(\Xi_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}})
and similarly for the other two direct summands. We similarly denote the induced subquotients on the specializations of
\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}^{\dagger}
(that is,
\mathscr{F}^{i}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}}^{gh}
, etc.).

Consider the class
\kappa_{1,{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{(3)}
defined in (
6.3
) for the choice of level-
N
test vectors
(\underline{\breve{{\mathbf{f}}}},\underline{\breve{g}},\underline{\breve{h}})
given by Theorem
7.1
and let
\kappa({\mathbf{f}},g,h)\in H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})
be the image of this class via the morphism obained from the augmentation map
\mathcal{O}[R_{1}]\rightarrow\mathcal{O}
. By
[
BSV22
, Cor. 8.2]
, the image of
\kappa({\mathbf{f}},g,h)
under the restriction map at
p
is contained in

H^{1}_{\bal}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger}):={\rm im}\bigl(H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathscr{F}^{2}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})\rightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})\bigr).

It is easily seen that this map is an injection, so we may and will view
{\rm res}_{p}(\kappa({\mathbf{f}},g,h))
as a class in
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathscr{F}^{2}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})
. Let

{\rm pr}_{({\mathbf{f}},g,h)}:\mathscr{F}^{2}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger}\longrightarrow\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}}^{gh}

be the map induced by the projection onto the first direct summand in (
7.4
).
The “reciprocity law” from
[
BSV22
,
DR22
]
recalled in Theorem
7.4
below relates the image of
{\rm res}_{p}(\kappa({\mathbf{f}},g,h))
under the natural projection

{\rm pr}_{({\mathbf{f}},g,h)*}:H^{1}_{\bal}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}}^{gh})

to the triple product
p
-adic
L
-function of
\lx@sectionsign\ref{subsec:Lp}
.
Recall that
\xi\in\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}
denotes a generator of the congruence ideal of
{\mathbf{f}}
.

Proposition 7.3
.

There is an injective
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}
-module homomorphism with pseudo-null cokernel

\mathfrak{Log}^{\xi}:H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}}^{gh})\longrightarrow\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}

characterized by the following interpolation property: for all
\mathfrak{Z}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}}^{gh})
and all classical points
x\in\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda_{\mathbf{f}}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
of weight
k\geq l+m
with
k\equiv r_{1}+2\pmod{2(p-1)}
we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathfrak{Log}^{\xi}(\mathfrak{Z})(x)}{\xi_{x}}

\displaystyle=(p-1)\alpha_{k}\biggl(1-\frac{\beta_{k}\alpha_{g}\alpha_{h}}{p^{c}}\biggr)\biggl(1-\frac{\alpha_{k}\beta_{g}\beta_{h}}{p^{c}}\biggr)^{-1}

\displaystyle\quad\times\begin{cases}\frac{(-1)^{c-k}}{(c-k)!}\cdot\left\langle{\rm log}_{p}(\mathfrak{Z}_{k}),\eta_{{\mathbf{f}}_{k}}\otimes\omega_{{\mathbf{g}}_{l}}\otimes\omega_{{\mathbf{h}}_{m}}\right\rangle_{\rm dR},&\textrm{if $l-m<k<l+m$,}\\[5.0pt]
(k-c-1)!\cdot\left\langle{\rm exp}_{p}^{*}(\mathfrak{Z}_{k}),\eta_{{\mathbf{f}}_{k}}\otimes\omega_{{\mathbf{g}}_{l}}\otimes\omega_{{\mathbf{h}}_{m}}\right\rangle_{\rm dR},&\textrm{if $k\geq l+m$,}\end{cases}

where
c=(k+l+m-2)/2
.

Proof.

The construction of
\mathfrak{Log}^{\xi}
will follow by specializing the three-variable
p
-adic regulator constructed in
[
BSV22
, §7.1]
(building on a generalization of the construction in
[
LZ14
]
given by Kings–Loeffler–Zerbes
[
KLZ17
]
).

Let
\vartheta_{gh}:\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}\rightarrow\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}
be the map given by reduction modulo
(\bm{l}-l,\bm{m}-m)
. This induces isomorphisms

\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}^{\dagger}\otimes_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}}\Lambda_{\mathbf{f}}\cong\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger},\quad\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}}^{{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}\otimes_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}}\Lambda_{\mathbf{f}}\cong\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}}^{gh},

and a natural map

\vartheta_{gh*}:H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}}^{{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}})\otimes_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}}\Lambda_{\mathbf{f}}\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}}^{gh}).

This map is clearly injective, and its surjectivity can be shown easily by an application of local Tate duality and the Ramanujan–Petersson conjecture (
cf.
proof of
[
BSV22
, (154)]
). Letting

\mathscr{L}_{{\mathbf{f}}}^{\xi}:H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}}^{{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}})\longrightarrow\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}

be the
p
-adic regulator
\mathscr{L}_{{\mathbf{f}}}
defined as in
[
BSV22
, Prop. 7.3]
and multiplied by
\xi
, the map defined by the composition

\mathfrak{Log}^{\xi}:H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}}^{gh})\xrightarrow{\vartheta_{gh*}^{-1}}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}}^{{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}})\otimes_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}}\Lambda_{\mathbf{f}}\xrightarrow{\mathscr{L}^{\xi}_{{\mathbf{f}}}\otimes{\rm id}}\Lambda_{\mathbf{f}}

satisfies the interpolation properties in the statement of the proposition.

It remains to see that
\mathfrak{Log}^{\xi}
is injective with pseudo-null cokernel. By definition, we have

\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}}^{{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}=\mathbb{U}_{{\mathbf{f}}}^{{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}(\epsilon_{\rm cyc}\kappa_{{\mathbf{f}}}^{-1/2}\kappa_{{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}^{1/2}),

where
\mathbb{U}_{{\mathbf{f}}}^{{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}
is an unramified
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-module on which an arithmetic Frobenius
{\rm Fr}_{p}
acts as multiplication by
\chi_{f}^{-1}(p)a_{p}({\mathbf{f}})a_{p}({\mathbf{g}})^{-1}a_{p}({\mathbf{h}})^{-1}
, and
\mathscr{L}_{{\mathbf{f}}}

is obtained by specializing the four-variable
p
-adic regulator map in
[
KLZ17
, Thm. 8.2.3]
for the module
\mathbb{U}_{{\mathbf{f}}}^{{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}
, paired against the differential
\eta_{{\mathbf{f}}}\otimes\omega_{{\mathbf{g}}}\otimes\omega_{{\mathbf{h}}}
.
In light of
[
KLZ17
, Rmk. 8.2.4]
, the fact that
\mathfrak{Log}^{\xi}
has the above properties can therefore be deduced from the vanishing of
H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{U}_{{\mathbf{f}}}^{gh})
, where
\mathbb{U}_{{\mathbf{f}}}^{gh}
is the image of
\mathbb{U}_{{\mathbf{f}}}^{{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}
under
\vartheta_{gh}
.
∎

Theorem 7.4

(Reciprocity law)
.

We have the following equality

\mathfrak{Log}^{\xi}({\rm res}_{p}(\kappa({\mathbf{f}},g,h)))=\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\xi}(\underline{\breve{{\mathbf{f}}}},\underline{\breve{g}},\underline{\breve{h}}).

Proof.

This is the specialization of the three-variable reciprocity law of Theorem A in
[
BSV22
]
to
({\mathbf{f}},g,h)
(see also
[
DR22
, Thm. 10]
).
∎

7.3.
Selmer groups and formulation of the main conjectures

Let
({\mathbf{f}},g,h)
be as in the preceding subsection. Throughout the rest of this section, we assume that hypotheses (a)–(c) in Theorem
7.1
hold, so the
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{p}({\mathbf{f}},g,h)
in Definition
7.2
is available.

Recall the
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-stable rank-four
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}
-submodule
\mathscr{F}^{2}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}^{\dagger}\subset\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}^{{\dagger}}
in
(\ref{eq:+})
, and set

\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}^{f}:=\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{g}}}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{h}}}(\Xi_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}).

As before, we let
\mathscr{F}^{2}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}
and
\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{f}
denote the corresponding specializations.

Fix a finite set
\Sigma
of places of
\mathbb{Q}
containing
\infty
and the primes dividing
Np
, and let
\mathbb{Q}^{\Sigma}
be the maximal extension of
\mathbb{Q}
unramified outside
\Sigma
.

Definition 7.5
.

For
\mathcal{L}\in\{{\rm bal},\mathcal{F}\}
define the Selmer group
\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})
by

\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})=\ker\bigg(H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}^{\Sigma}/\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})\longrightarrow\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})}{H^{1}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})}\bigg),

where

H_{\mathcal{L}}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})=\begin{cases}\ker\bigl(H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}}/\mathscr{F}^{2}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})\bigr)&\textrm{if $\mathcal{L}={\rm bal}$},\\[5.0pt]
\ker\bigl(H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}}/\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{f})\bigr)&\textrm{if $\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{F}$}.\end{cases}

We call
\mathrm{Sel}_{\rm bal}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})
(resp.
\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})
) the
balanced
(resp.
f
-unbalanced
) Selmer group.

Remark 7.6
.

The pairs

\bigl(\mathscr{F}^{2}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger},\{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 2}\;\colon\;l-m<k<l+m\}\bigr),\quad\bigl(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{f},\{k\in\mathbb{Z}\;\colon\;k\geq l+m\}\bigr)

satisfy the
Panchishkin condition
in
[
Gre94
]
. Thus
{\rm Sel}_{\bal}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})
and
{\rm Sel}_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})
may be viewed as instances of Greenberg’s Selmer groups attached to different ranges of critical specializations of
\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger}
.

Let

\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger}={\rm Hom}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger},\mu_{p^{\infty}}).

Then for
\mathcal{L}\in\{\bal,\mathcal{F}\}
, we define the Selmer groups
{\rm Sel}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})
as above,
taking
H^{1}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})
to be the orthogonal complement of
H^{1}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})
under the local Tate duality

H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})\times H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})\longrightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p},

and set

X_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}}):=\Hom_{\text{cont}}(\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}}),\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}).

In light of Remark
7.6
, the next conjecture may be viewed as an instance of the Iwasawa–Greenberg main conjectures
[
Gre94
]
. In the two formulations below, we also assume conditions (b) and (c) from Theorem
7.1
, so that the
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{p}({\mathbf{f}},g,h)
in (
7.2
) is defined.

Conjecture 7.7

(IMC “with
p
-adic
L
-functions”)
.

The modules
\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})
and
X_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})
are both
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}
-torsion, and

\Char_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}}(X_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}}))=(L_{p}({\mathbf{f}},g,h))

in
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

Remark 7.8
.

An integral formulation of the equality of ideals in Conjecture
7.7
would in general involve certain Tamagawa factors, accounting for the fact that the construction of
L_{p}({\mathbf{f}},g,h)
uses Hida’s congruence number, while by definition the classes in the Selmer group
X_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})
are trivial at the places
v\in\Sigma\smallsetminus\{p,\infty\}
, rather than just unramified (
cf.

[
PW11
]
).

Under the local root number hypothesis (b) in Theorem
7.1
, for all arithmetic point
x\in\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda_{\mathbf{f}}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
of even weight
k\geq 2
with
l-m<k<l-m
the sign in the functional equation for
L(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}_{x}gh}^{\dagger},s)
is
-1
, so that the central value
L(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}_{x}gh}^{\dagger},0)
vanishes. Therefore, in the spirit of Perrin-Riou’s main conjecture
[
PR87
, Conj. B]
in the setting of Heegner points, a natural formulation of the Iwasawa main conjecture for
{\rm Sel}_{\bal}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})
takes the following form.

Note that it follows from
[
BSV22
, Cor. 8.2]
that
\kappa({\mathbf{f}},g,h)
lands in
\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})
.

Conjecture 7.9

(IMC “without
p
-adic
L
-functions”)
.

Suppose
\kappa({\mathbf{f}},g,h)\in\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})
is not
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}
-torsion. Then the modules
\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})
and
X_{\bal}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})
have both rank one, and

\Char_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}}(X_{\bal}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})_{\tors})=\Char_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})}{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}\cdot\kappa({\mathbf{f}},g,h)}\right)^{2}

in
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, where the subscript
{\rm tors}
denotes the
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}
-torsion submodule.

Remark 7.10
.

Working under different hypotheses on the local signs ensuring that
L(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}_{k}gh}^{\dagger},s)
has sign
+1
(rather than
-1
) for weights
k\geq 2
with
l-m<k<l-m
, the Iwasawa main conjecture would relate the characteristic ideal of
X_{\bal}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})

to the balanced triple product
p
-adic
L
-function constructed in
[
Hsi21
, Thm. B]
(see also
[
GS20
]
), rather than diagonal classes. In this setting, the
f
-unbalanced Selmer group
\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})
should have
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}
-rank one, but the expected non-torsion Selmer class seems to not have been constructed yet.

7.4.
Equivalence of the formulations

In this subsection we show that the two formulations of the Iwasawa main conjecture in the previous subsection are essentially equivalent, focusing on the case where
{\mathbf{f}}
is a CM Hida family
1
1

1

This case will suffice for our applications in this paper, and makes some of the arguments simpler, but we expect the equivalence to hold in general.
as in
\lx@sectionsign\ref{subsec:Iw}
.
Similar equivalences between IMC “with” and “without”
p
-adic
L
-functions appear in
[
Kat04
, §17]
,
[
KLZ17
, §11]
and, in a setting more germane to ours,
[
Wan20
]
and
[
Cas17
, Appendix]
.

The following intermediate Selmer groups will allow us to bridge between
{\rm Sel}_{\bal}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})
and
{\rm Sel}_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})
in the comparison. Set

\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{f\cap+}=\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{f}\cap\mathscr{F}^{2}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger},\quad\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{f\cup+}=\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{f}+\mathscr{F}^{2}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger},

which are
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-stable
\Lambda_{\mathbf{f}}
-submodules of
\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger}
of ranks
3
and
5
, respectively. Define
\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})
for
\mathcal{L}\in\{\mathcal{F}\cap+,\mathcal{F}\cup+\}
by the same recipe as in Definition
7.5
, with

H_{\mathcal{L}}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})=\begin{cases}\ker\bigl(H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})\rightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}}/\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{f\cap+})\bigr)&\textrm{if $\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{F}\cap+$},\\[5.0pt]
\ker\bigl(H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})\rightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}}/\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{f\cup+})\bigr)&\textrm{if $\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{F}\cup+$}.\end{cases}

We define the Selmer groups
\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}\cap+}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})
and
\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}\cup+}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})
taking
H_{\mathcal{F}\cap+}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})
and
H_{\mathcal{F}\cup+}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})
to be the orthogonal complements of
H_{\mathcal{F}\cup+}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})
and
H_{\mathcal{F}\cap+}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})
, respectively. As in the preceding section, we also define the corresponding
X_{\mathcal{F}\cap+}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})
and
X_{\mathcal{F}\cup+}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})
.

Throughout this subsection, we keep the setting from
\lx@sectionsign\ref{subsec:Iw}
. In particular,
{\mathbf{f}}\in\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}[[q]]
is the CM Hida family in
(\ref{eq:CM-F})
associated with the Hecke character
\psi
of conductor
\mathfrak{f}
. In addition, we assume conditions (b) and (c) from Theorem
7.1
, so the
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{p}({\mathbf{f}},g,h)\in\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}
is defined, and let
\kappa({\mathbf{f}},g,h)\in H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})
be as above.

For every height one prime
\mathfrak{Q}
of
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}
away from
p
, let
S_{\mathfrak{Q}}
be the integral closure of
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}/\mathfrak{Q}

and let
\Phi_{\mathfrak{Q}}
be the fraction field of
S_{\mathfrak{Q}}
. Let
\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh,\mathfrak{Q}}^{\dagger}
be the extension of scalars of
\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}}/\mathfrak{Q}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger}
to
S_{\mathfrak{Q}}
,
and let
\mathbb{A}^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh,\mathfrak{Q}}=\Hom(\mathbb{V}^{\dagger}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh,\mathfrak{Q}},\mu_{p^{\infty}})
.
Following
[
MR04
]
, define

(7.5)

H^{1}_{\underline{\rm bal}}(\mathbb{Q}_{v},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh,\mathfrak{Q}}^{{\dagger}}):=\begin{cases}\ker\bigl(H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{v},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh,\mathfrak{Q}}^{{\dagger}})\rightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{v}^{\rm nr},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh,\mathfrak{Q}}^{{\dagger}}\otimes\Phi_{\mathfrak{Q}})\bigr),&\textrm{if $v\nmid p$,}\\[5.0pt]
\ker\bigl(H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{v},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh,\mathfrak{Q}}^{{\dagger}})\rightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{v},(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh,\mathfrak{Q}}^{{\dagger}}/\mathscr{F}^{2}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh,\mathfrak{Q}}^{\dagger})\otimes\Phi_{\mathfrak{Q}})\bigr),&\textrm{if $v\mid p$,}\end{cases}

and let
H^{1}_{\underline{\bal}}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh,\mathfrak{Q}}^{{\dagger}})
be the associated Selmer group. Taking
H^{1}_{\underline{\bal}}(\mathbb{Q}_{v},\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh,\mathfrak{Q}}^{{\dagger}})
to be the orthogonal complement of
H^{1}_{\underline{\bal}}(\mathbb{Q}_{v},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh,\mathfrak{Q}}^{{\dagger}})
under local Tate duality, we define the Selmer group
H^{1}_{\underline{\bal}}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh,\mathfrak{Q}}^{{\dagger}})
similarly.

Define
\mathbb{V}_{g,h}^{\psi}=\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}}(\bm{\kappa}_{\text{ac}}^{-1})\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}T_{g,h}^{\psi}(\kappa_{\ac}^{r_{1}/2})
and let
\mathbb{A}_{g,h}^{\psi}=\Hom((\mathbb{V}_{g,h}^{\psi})^{c},\mu_{p^{\infty}})
, where
(\mathbb{V}_{g,h}^{\psi})^{c}
denotes
\mathbb{V}_{g,h}^{\psi}
with the
G_{K}
-action twisted by complex conjugation.
Note that
\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}}=\text{Ind}_{K}^{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{V}_{g,h}^{\psi}
, so we can define Selmer conditions for
\mathbb{V}_{g,h}^{\psi}
using Shapiro’s lemma and for
\mathbb{A}_{g,h}^{\psi}
by duality. Define
\mathbb{A}_{g,h,\mathfrak{Q}}^{\psi}=\Hom((\mathbb{V}_{g,h,\mathfrak{Q}}^{\psi})^{c},\mu_{p^{\infty}})
. We have natural maps

(7.6)
