\mathbb{V}_{g,h}^{\psi}/\mathfrak{Q}\mathbb{V}_{g,h}^{\psi}\rightarrow\mathbb{V}_{g,h,\mathfrak{Q}}^{\psi},\quad\mathbb{A}_{g,h,\mathfrak{Q}}^{\psi}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}_{g,h}^{\psi}[\mathfrak{Q}]

preserving both the
G_{K}
and the
\Lambda
-modules structure in the same way as in
[
How04
, p. 1461]
. Note that in the quotient
\mathbb{V}_{g,h}^{\psi}/\mathfrak{Q}\mathbb{V}_{g,h}^{\psi}
and in the submodule
\mathbb{A}_{g,h}^{\psi}[\mathfrak{Q}]
we can define Selmer conditions by propagating the balanced conditions for
\mathbb{V}_{g,h}^{\psi}
and
\mathbb{A}_{g,h}^{\psi}
, respectively, and we denote these conditions in the same way.

Lemma 7.11
.

For every height one prime
\mathfrak{Q}\subset\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}
as above and every place
v
of
K
, the maps
(\ref{eq:spQ})
induce natural maps

\displaystyle H^{1}_{\bal}(K_{v},\mathbb{V}_{gh}^{\psi}/\mathfrak{Q}\mathbb{V}_{gh}^{\psi})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\underline{\bal}}(K_{v},\mathbb{V}_{gh,\mathfrak{Q}}^{\psi}),

\displaystyle H^{1}_{\underline{\bal}}(K_{v},\mathbb{A}_{gh,\mathfrak{Q}}^{\psi})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\bal}(K_{v},\mathbb{A}_{gh}^{\psi}[\mathfrak{Q}])

with finite kernel and cokernel, of order bounded by constants depending only on
[S_{\mathfrak{Q}}:\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}/\mathfrak{Q}]
.

Proof.

For the primes
v\nmid p
, the same argument as in the proof of
[
MR04
, Lem. 5.3.13]
applies, so it remains to consider the case
v\mid p
. Put

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}(T_{g,h}^{\psi})

\displaystyle=(T_{g}^{+}\otimes T_{h}+T_{g}\otimes T_{h}^{+})(\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}^{-1})(-1-r),

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}}^{+}(T_{g,h}^{\psi})

\displaystyle=(T_{g}^{+}\otimes T_{h}^{+})(\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}^{-1})(-1-r).

Under the isomorphism
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})\cong H^{1}(K,\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}}(\bm{\kappa}_{\text{ac}}^{-1})\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}T_{g,h}^{\psi}(\kappa_{\ac}^{r_{1}/2}))
coming from Shapiro’s lemma, the balanced local condition
H^{1}_{\rm bal}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})
corresponds to

H^{1}(K_{\mathfrak{p}},\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}}(\bm{\kappa}_{\text{ac}}^{-1})\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}(T_{g,h}^{\psi})(\kappa_{\ac}^{r_{1}/2}))\oplus H^{1}(K_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}}(\bm{\kappa}_{\text{ac}}^{-1})\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathcal{F}_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}}^{+}(T_{g,h}^{\psi})(\kappa_{\ac}^{r_{1}/2})).

Let
A_{g}^{-}=T_{g}^{-}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, and define
A_{g}^{+}
,
A_{h}^{-}
and
A_{h}^{+}
similarly. Arguing as in the proof of
[
How04
, Lem. 2.2.7]
, we reduce to showing that the groups

H^{0}(K_{\infty,\mathfrak{p}},(A_{g}^{-}\otimes A_{h}^{-})(\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}^{-1}\kappa_{\ac}^{r_{1}/2})(-1-r)),\quad H^{0}(K_{\infty,\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},(A_{g}^{+}\otimes A_{h}^{-})(\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}^{-1}\kappa_{\ac}^{r_{1}/2})(-1-r))

are both finite, which follows from the fact that
\alpha_{g}\alpha_{h}\psi(\mathfrak{p})/p^{k-1}\neq 1
and
\beta_{g}\alpha_{h}\psi(\overline{\mathfrak{p}})\neq 1
, and this is a consequence of the Ramanujan–Petersson conjecture since we are assuming that
p\nmid N
. Note that the other pieces in the quotient decomposition can be treated similarly. This yields the required bounds on the kernel and cokernel of the first map in the statement of the lemma, and the result for the second map follows as well by local duality.
∎

Let
\Sigma_{\Lambda}
be the set of height one primes of
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}
consisting of
p
and those for which either
H^{2}(\mathbb{Q}^{\Sigma}/\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})[\mathfrak{Q}]
is infinite or
H^{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})[\mathfrak{Q}]
is infinite. Since
H^{2}(\mathbb{Q}^{\Sigma}/\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})
and
H^{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})
are both finitely generated
\Lambda
-modules, the set
\Sigma_{\Lambda}
is finite.

Proposition 7.12
.

For every height one prime
\mathfrak{Q}\not\in\Sigma_{\Lambda}
, the maps (
7.6
) induce natural maps

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})/\mathfrak{Q}\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sel}_{\underline{\bal}}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh,\mathfrak{Q}}^{{\dagger}}),

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sel}_{\underline{\bal}}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh,\mathfrak{Q}}^{{\dagger}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})[\mathfrak{Q}]

with finite kernel and cokernel bounded by a constant depending only on
[S_{\mathfrak{Q}}:\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}/\mathfrak{Q}]
.

Proof.

This follows from Lemma
7.11
as in the proof of
[
MR04
, Prop. 5.3.14]
(see also
[
How04
, Lem. 2.2.8]
and
[
How07
, Lem. 3.2.10]
).
∎

For every height one prime
\mathfrak{Q}\subset\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}
as above, let
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{Q}}=(\pi_{\mathfrak{Q}})
be the maximal ideal of
S_{\mathfrak{Q}}
.

Lemma 7.13
.

Assume that there is a perfect pairing
T_{g,h}^{\psi}\times T_{g,h}^{\psi}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(1)
such that
\langle x^{\sigma},y^{c\sigma c}\rangle=\langle x,y\rangle^{\sigma}
for all
x,y\in T_{g,h}^{\psi}
and for all
\sigma\in G_{K}
, where
c
stands for complex conjugation. The following hold:

(1)

{\rm rank}_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}}\,\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})={\rm rank}_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}}\,X_{\bal}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})
.

(2)

{\rm rank}_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}}\,\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})={\rm rank}_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}}\,X_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})
.

(3)

{\rm rank}_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}}\,X_{\mathcal{F}\cup+}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})=1+{\rm rank}_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}}\,X_{\mathcal{F}\cap+}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})
, and

\Char_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}}(X_{\mathcal{F}\cup+}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})_{\tors})=\Char_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}}(X_{\mathcal{F}\cap+}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})_{\rm tors}),

in
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

Proof.

For part (1), it suffices to show that for all height one primes
\mathfrak{Q}\subset\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}
with
\mathfrak{Q}\not\in\Sigma_{\Lambda}
, the modules
\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})/\mathfrak{Q}\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})
and
\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})[\mathfrak{Q}]
have the same rank over
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}/\mathfrak{Q}
. Since
\mathrm{Sel}_{\underline{\bal}}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh,\mathfrak{Q}}^{{\dagger}})
is the
\pi_{\mathfrak{Q}}
-adic Tate module of
\mathrm{Sel}_{\underline{\bal}}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh,\mathfrak{Q}}^{{\dagger}})
(indeed, this is a consequence of
[
How04
, Lem. 1.3.3]
since
\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh,\mathfrak{Q}}^{{\dagger}}\cong\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh,\mathfrak{Q}}^{{\dagger}}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}
), the result thus follows from Proposition
7.12
.

For part (2), under the isomorphism
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})\cong H^{1}(K,\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}}(\bm{\kappa}_{\text{ac}}^{-1})\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}T_{g,h}^{\psi}(\kappa_{\ac}^{r_{1}/2}))
the
f
-unbalanced local condition
H^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})
corresponds to

H^{1}(K_{\mathfrak{p}},\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}}(\bm{\kappa}_{\text{ac}}^{-1})\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}T_{g,h}^{\psi})\oplus\{0\}

and hence an analogue of Lemma
7.11
for the
f
-unbalanced Selmer groups follows from the finiteness of
H^{0}(K_{\infty,\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},A_{g}\otimes A_{h}(\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}^{-1}\kappa_{\ac}^{r_{1}/2})(-1-r))
. By the same reason as above, this yields the equality of ranks in part (2).

Finally, for the proof of part (3) we can argue similarly as in
[
AH06
, Thm. 1.2.2]
. Keeping with the above notations, let
\mathrm{Sel}_{\underline{\mathcal{F}\cup+}}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh,\mathfrak{Q}}^{\dagger})
and
\mathrm{Sel}_{\underline{\mathcal{F}\cap+}}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh,\mathfrak{Q}}^{\dagger})
be the Selmer groups defined by the obvious analogues of (
7.5
), so from another application of the argument in Lemma
7.11
we obtain natural maps

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sel}_{\underline{\mathcal{F}\cup+}}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh,\mathfrak{Q}}^{{\dagger}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}\cup+}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})[\mathfrak{Q}],

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sel}_{\underline{\mathcal{F}\cap+}}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh,\mathfrak{Q}}^{{\dagger}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}\cap+}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})[\mathfrak{Q}]

with finite kernel and cokernel bounded by a constant depending only on
[S_{\mathfrak{Q}}:\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}/\mathfrak{Q}]
. Since the local condition
\mathcal{F}\cap+
is the orthogonal complement of
\mathcal{F}\cup+
under the local Tate pairing at
p
induced by the self-duality of
\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger}
, from
[
MR04
, Thm. 4.1.13]
we obtain

(7.7)

\mathrm{Sel}_{\underline{\mathcal{F}\cup+}}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh,\mathfrak{Q}}^{{\dagger}})[\pi_{\mathfrak{Q}}^{i}]\cong(\Phi_{\mathfrak{Q}}/S_{\mathfrak{Q}})^{r}[\pi_{\mathfrak{Q}}^{i}]\oplus\mathrm{Sel}_{\underline{\mathcal{F}\cap+}}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh,\mathfrak{Q}}^{{\dagger}})[\pi_{\mathfrak{Q}}^{i}]

for all
i
, where
r
is given (by the Greenberg–Wiles formula in
[
MR04
, Prop. 2.3.5]
) by

{\rm corank}_{S_{\mathfrak{Q}}}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh,\mathfrak{Q}}^{f\cup+})-{\rm corank}_{S_{\mathfrak{Q}}}H^{0}(\mathbb{R},\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh,\mathfrak{Q}}^{\dagger}),

so
r=5-4=1
. The proof of part (3) now follows from (
7.7
) as in
[
AH06
, Lem. 1.2.6]
.
∎

Remark 7.14
.

The existence of the pairing in the previous lemma is not too restrictive. In particular, this holds automatically if
g
and
h
are non-Eisenstein.

We are now ready to establish that both formulations of the Iwasawa main conjecture are equivalent.

Theorem 7.15
.

Keep the assumptions of the previous lemma and suppose
\kappa({\mathbf{f}},g,h)
is not
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}
-torsion. Then the following are equivalent:

(1)

{\rm rank}_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}}\,\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})={\rm rank}_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}}\,X_{\bal}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})=1
;

(2)

{\rm rank}_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}}\,\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})={\rm rank}_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}}\,X_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})=0
,

and, in that case, we have
\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})=\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}\cup+}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})
and

\Char_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}}(X_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}}))\cdot\Char_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})}{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}\cdot\kappa({\mathbf{f}},g,h)}\right)^{2}=\Char_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}}(X_{\bal}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})_{\tors})\cdot(L_{p}({{\mathbf{f}}},{g},{h}))

in
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. In particular, Conjecture
7.7
and Conjecture
7.9
are equivalent.

Proof.

The Poitou–Tate global duality gives rise to the exact sequence

(7.8)

\begin{split}0\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}\cap+}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})&\overset{{{\rm res}_{p}}}{\longrightarrow}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}}^{gh})\\
&\longrightarrow X_{\mathcal{F}\cup+}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})\longrightarrow X_{\bal}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})\longrightarrow 0.\end{split}

Assume that
\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})
and
X_{\bal}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})
have both
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}
-rank one. Since
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}}^{gh})
has
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}
-rank one, from (
7.8
) and Theorem
7.4
we see that
\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}\cap+}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})
is
\Lambda_{\mathbf{f}}
-torsion and
X_{\mathcal{F}\cup+}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})
has
\Lambda_{\mathbf{f}}
-rank one. By Lemma
7.13
(3), it follows that
X_{\mathcal{F}\cap+}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})
is
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}
-torsion, and from the exact sequence

(7.9)

\begin{split}0\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}\cup+}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})&\overset{{{\rm res}_{p}}}{\longrightarrow}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}}^{gh})\\
&\longrightarrow X_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})\longrightarrow X_{\mathcal{F}\cap+}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})\longrightarrow 0\end{split}

we get that
X_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})
and
\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})
are both
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}
-torsion by Lemma
7.13
(2). This proves the implication
{\rm(1)}\Rightarrow{\rm(2)}
in the statement of the theorem, and the converse is shown similarly. Moreover, from (
7.9
) we see that
{\rm rank}_{\Lambda_{\mathbf{f}}}\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}\cup+}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})=1
, and hence the quotient
\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}\cup+}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})/\mathrm{Sel}_{\rm bal}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})
is a torsion
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}
-module injecting into
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}^{f\cup+}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}/\mathscr{F}^{2}\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})
; since this is
\Lambda_{\mathbf{f}}
-torsion free by Proposition
7.3
, it follows that

(7.10)

\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})=\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}\cup+}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger}).

Now suppose that either (1) or (2) in the statement of theorem holds. Since
\bar{\rho}_{{\mathbf{f}}}
is absolutely irreducible by our hypotheses, the module
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}^{\Sigma}/\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})
is
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}
-torsion free by
[
PR00
, §1.3.3]
. Being
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}
-torsion, it follows that the module
\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}\cap+}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})
vanishes, and therefore from (
7.8
) we deduce the exact sequence

(7.11)

0\longrightarrow\frac{\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})}{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}\cdot\kappa({\mathbf{f}},g,h)}\longrightarrow\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}}^{gh})}{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}\cdot p_{{\mathbf{f}}*}({\rm res}_{p}(\kappa({\mathbf{f}},g,h)))}\longrightarrow{\rm coker}({\rm res}_{p})\longrightarrow 0.

Together with Theorem
7.4
it follows that

(7.12)

\Char_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})}{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}\cdot\kappa({\mathbf{f}},g,h)}\right)\cdot\Char_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}}({\rm coker}({\rm res}_{p}))=(\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\xi}(\underline{\breve{{\mathbf{f}}}},\underline{\breve{g}},\underline{\breve{h}})).

On the other hand, in light of (
7.10
), from (
7.8
) and (
7.9
) we deduce exact sequences

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow{\rm coker}({\rm res}_{p})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow X_{\mathcal{F}\cup+}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})\longrightarrow X_{\bal}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})\longrightarrow 0,

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow{\rm coker}({\rm res}_{p})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow X_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})\longrightarrow X_{\mathcal{F}\cap+}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})\longrightarrow 0.

Taking characteristic ideals, these imply

(7.13)

\displaystyle\Char_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}}(X_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})

\displaystyle=\Char_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}}(X_{\mathcal{F}\cap+}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}}))\cdot\Char_{\Lambda_{\mathbf{f}}}({\rm coker}({\rm res}_{p}))

\displaystyle=\Char_{\Lambda_{\mathbf{f}}}(X_{\mathcal{F}\cup+}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})_{\rm tors})\cdot\Char_{\Lambda_{\mathbf{f}}}({\rm coker}({\rm res}_{p}))

\displaystyle=\Char_{\Lambda_{\mathbf{f}}}(X_{\bal}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})_{\rm tors})\cdot\Char_{\Lambda_{\mathbf{f}}}({\rm coker}({\rm res}_{p}))^{2},

using Lemma
7.13
(3) for the second equality. Multiplying (
7.13
) by the square of a generator of the characteristic ideal of
\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})/\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}\cdot\kappa({\mathbf{f}},g,h)
and using (
7.12
), the result follows.
∎

8.
Anticyclotomic Euler systems

In this section we highlight results from the recent work of Jetchev–Nekovář–Skinner
[
JNS
]
, where a general theory of Euler systems germane to
[
Rub00
]
is developed in the anticyclotomic setting.

8.1.
The general theory

Let
K
be an imaginary quadratic field and let
p
be an odd prime. If
\mathfrak{n}
is an integral prime ideal of
K
, we denote by
K(\mathfrak{n})^{\circ}
the ray class field of conductor
\mathfrak{n}
; as in the previous sections,
we write
K(\mathfrak{n})
for the maximal
p
-subextension in
K(\mathfrak{n})^{\circ}
. For any positive integer
n
, we denote by
K[n]
the maximal
p
-subextension in the ring class field of
K
of conductor
n
.
We denote by
K_{\infty}
the anticyclotomic
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-extension of
K
.

Let
E
be a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
with ring of integers
\mathcal{O}
and maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}
. Let
T
be a free
\mathcal{O}
-module of finite rank endowed with a continuous
G_{K}
-action unramified outside a finite set of primes, and let
V=T\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}E
. Assume that there exists a non-degenerate symmetric
\mathcal{O}
-bilinear pairing

\langle\,,\,\rangle\,:\,T\times T\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}(1)

such that
\langle x^{\sigma},y^{c\sigma c}\rangle=\langle x,y\rangle^{\sigma}
for all
x,y\in T
and
\sigma\in G_{K}
, where
c
is complex conjugation. Thus
V^{c}\simeq V^{\vee}(1)
, where
V^{c}
denotes the representation
V
with the
G_{K}
-action twisted by
c
, and, if the above pairing is perfect, we also have
T^{c}\simeq T^{\vee}(1)
. We also define the
G_{K}
-module
A=V/T
.

If
L
is a finite extension of
K
and
v
is a finite place of
L
, we write
\overline{v}=v^{c}
. Then, the pairing above induces a local pairing

H^{1}(L_{v},V)\times H^{1}(L_{\overline{v}},V)\longrightarrow E,

and similarly replacing
V
by
T
and
E
by
\mathcal{O}
. The pair of compatible maps
G_{L_{v}}\rightarrow G_{L_{\overline{v}}}
and
V\rightarrow V^{c}
defined by
\sigma\mapsto c\sigma c
and
w\mapsto w
, respectively, induces an isomorphism
H^{1}(L_{\overline{v}},V)\cong H^{1}(L_{v},V^{c})\cong H^{1}(L_{v},V^{\vee}(1))
whereby the above local pairing is just the natural cup-product pairing.

For the results we shall discuss, we consider two different types of “big image” hypotheses, (HW) for the weaker ones, and (HS) for the stronger ones.

Hypothesis

(HW)
.

(1)

V
is absolutely irreducible as a
G_{K}
-representation.

(2)

There exists an element
\sigma_{0}\in\mathrm{Gal\,}(\bar{K}/K(1)^{\circ}K(\mu_{p^{\infty}}))
such that the
E
-dimension of
V/(\sigma_{0}-1)V
is one.

Hypothesis

(HS)
.

(1’)

The residual representation
\bar{T}=T/\mathfrak{m}T
is absolutely irreducible.

(2’)

There exists an element
\sigma_{0}\in\mathrm{Gal\,}(\bar{K}/K(p^{\infty})^{\circ})
such that
T/(\sigma_{0}-1)T\simeq\mathcal{O}
is a free
\mathcal{O}
-module of rank one.

(3’)

There exists an element
\tau_{0}\in G_{K}
such that
\tau_{0}-1
acts on
T
as multiplication by a unit
a_{\tau_{0}}\in\mathcal{O}^{\times}
with
a_{\tau_{0}}-1\in\mathcal{O}^{\times}
.

(4’)

The above pairing
T\times T\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}(1)
is perfect.

For each prime
\mathfrak{p}
of
K
above
p
, choose a
G_{K_{\mathfrak{p}}}
-stable
\mathcal{O}
-submodule
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}(T)
of
T
, and let
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-}(T)=T/\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}(T)
. We also define
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}(V)=\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}(T)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}E\subseteq V
and
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-}(V)=V/\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}(V)
. Let
L
be a finite extension of
K
. For each place
v
of
L
, we define a local condition

H^{1}_{\Gr}(L_{v},V)=\begin{cases}\ker\left(H^{1}(L_{v},V)\rightarrow H^{1}(L_{v}^{\text{nr}},V)\right)&\text{if }v\nmid p,\\[5.0pt]
\ker\left(H^{1}(L_{v},V)\rightarrow H^{1}(L_{v},\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-}(V))\right)&\text{if }v\mid\mathfrak{p}\text{ for some }\mathfrak{p}\mid p.\end{cases}

We define the
Greenberg Selmer group

\mathrm{Sel}_{\Gr}(L,V)=\ker\Big(H^{1}(L,V)\rightarrow\prod_{v}H^{1}(L_{v},V)/H^{1}_{\Gr}(L_{v},V)\Big),

where the product is over all finite places of
L
.

We also define local conditions for
T
and
A
by propagation of the local conditions for
V
, i.e., for each place
v
of
L
, we define

•

H^{1}_{\Gr}(L_{v},T)
as the preimage of
H^{1}_{\Gr}(L_{v},V)
by the map
H^{1}(L_{v},T)\rightarrow H^{1}(L_{v},V)
, and

•

H^{1}_{\Gr}(L_{v},A)
as the image of
H^{1}_{\Gr}(L_{v},V)
by the map
H^{1}(L_{v},V)\rightarrow H^{1}(L_{v},A)
,

and use these to define the Selmer groups
\mathrm{Sel}_{\Gr}(L,T)
and
\mathrm{Sel}_{\Gr}(L,A)
as above. Finally, for each positive integer
n
, we also put

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sel}_{\Gr}(K[np^{\infty}],T)

\displaystyle=\varprojlim_{r}\mathrm{Sel}_{\Gr}(K[np^{r}],T)\quad{\rm and}\quad\mathrm{Sel}_{\Gr}(K[np^{\infty}],A)=\varinjlim_{r}\mathrm{Sel}_{\Gr}(K[np^{r}],A),

where the limits are with respect to the corestriction and restriction maps, respectively, and we define

X_{\Gr}(K[np^{\infty}],A)=\Hom_{\mathrm{cont}}(\mathrm{Sel}_{\Gr}(K[np^{\infty}],A),\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}).

Let
\mathcal{N}
be an ideal of
K
divisible by
p
and all the primes at which
T
is ramified, and let
\mathcal{S}
be the set of all squarefree products of primes of
\mathbb{Q}
which
split
in
K
and are coprime to
\mathcal{N}
.

Definition 8.1
.

A
“split” anticyclotomic Euler system
for
(T,\{\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}(T)\}_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p},\mathcal{N})
is a collection of classes

\bm{\kappa}=\left\{\kappa_{n}\in\mathrm{Sel}_{\Gr}(K[n],T)\;\colon\;n\in\mathcal{S}\right\}

such that, whenever
q
is a rational prime and
n,nq\in\mathcal{S}
,

(8.1)

\cor_{K[nq]/K[n]}(\kappa_{nq})=P_{\mathfrak{q}}(\mathrm{Fr}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-1})\,\kappa_{n},

where
\mathfrak{q}
is any of the primes of
K
above
q
and

P_{\mathfrak{q}}(X)=\det(1-\mathrm{Fr}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-1}X|T^{\vee}(1))
.

Similarly, a
“split”
\Lambda
-adic anticyclotomic Euler system
for
(T,\{\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}(T)\}_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p},\mathcal{N})
is a collection of classes

\bm{\kappa}_{\infty}=\left\{\kappa_{n,\infty}\in\mathrm{Sel}_{\Gr}(K[np^{\infty}],T)\;\colon\;n\in\mathcal{S}\right\}

satisfying the previous norm relations. In this case, the classes

\kappa_{n}={\rm pr}_{K[n]}(\kappa_{n,\infty})\in\mathrm{Sel}_{\Gr}(K[n],T)

form an anticyclotomic Euler system in the previous sense, and we say that the Euler system
\bm{\kappa}=\{\kappa_{n}\}_{n}
extends along the anticyclotomic
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-extension.

A (
\Lambda
-adic) anticyclotomic Euler system for
(T,\{\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}(T)\}_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p})
is just a (
\Lambda
-adic) anticyclotomic Euler system for
(T,\{\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}(T)\}_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p},\mathcal{N})
for some
\mathcal{N}
as above. We shall usually drop
\{\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}(T)\}_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}
if there is no risk of confusion.

If
\bm{\kappa}
is an anticyclotomic Euler system for
T
, we define

\kappa_{0}:=\cor_{K[1]/K}(\kappa_{1})\in\mathrm{Sel}_{\Gr}(K,T).

If it extends along the anticyclotomic
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-extension, we similarly define

\kappa_{\infty}:=\cor_{K[1]/K}(\kappa_{1,\infty})\in\mathrm{Sel}_{\Gr}(K_{\infty},T),

where
\bm{\kappa}_{\infty}=\{\kappa_{n,\infty}\}
is the
\Lambda
-adic anticyclotomic Euler system extending
\bm{\kappa}
.

When we have an Euler system as above, we will be interested in ensuring that the following orthogonality hypothesis holds.

Hypothesis

(HO)
.

For all
n\in\mathcal{S}
and for all places
v
of
K[n]
above
p
, the local conditions
H^{1}_{\Gr}(K[n]_{v},V)
and
H^{1}_{\Gr}(K[n]_{\overline{v}},V)
are orthogonal complements under the local pairing

H^{1}(K[n]_{v},V)\times H^{1}(K[n]_{\overline{v}},V)\longrightarrow E.

Remark 8.2
.

The condition in hypothesis (HO) holds automatically for all places away from
p
, by
[
Rub00
, Prop. 1.4.2]
. Observe also that if (HO) holds, then for all
n\in\mathcal{S}
and for all places
v
of
K[n]
the local conditions
H^{1}_{\Gr}(K[n]_{v},T)
and
H^{1}_{\Gr}(K[n]_{\overline{v}},T)
are also orthogonal complements under the local pairing

H^{1}(K[n]_{v},T)\times H^{1}(K[n]_{\overline{v}},T)\longrightarrow\mathcal{O},

as follows easily from the definitions using
[
Rub00
, Prop. B.2.4]
and the commutative diagram

\textstyle{H^{1}(K[n]_{v},T)\times H^{1}(K[n]_{\overline{v}},T)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}(K[n]_{v},V)\times H^{1}(K[n]_{\overline{v}},V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E.}

We assume in the rest of this subsection that hypothesis (HO) holds for our choice of local conditions at
p
.

Theorem 8.3

(
[
JNS
]
)
.

Assume that
p
splits in
K
and that Hypothesis
(HW)
is satisfied, and let
\bm{\kappa}=\{\kappa_{n}\}_{n}
be an anticyclotomic Euler system for
T
which extends along the anticyclotomic
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-extension. If
\kappa_{0}\neq 0
, then the Selmer group
\mathrm{Sel}_{\Gr}(K,T)
has
\mathcal{O}
-rank one.

Remark 8.4
.

One can replace the assumptions that
p
splits in
K
and the Euler system extends along the anticyclotomic
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-extension by the assumption that there exists an element
\gamma\in G_{K}
fixing the extension
K(1)^{\circ}(\mu_{p^{\infty}},(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})^{1/p^{\infty}})
and such that
\gamma-1
acts invertibly on
V
.

Under the stronger Hypothesis (HS), granted the non-triviality of a
\Lambda
-adic anticyclotomic Euler system, the results of
[
JNS
]
yield a divisibility towards a corresponding Iwasawa main conjecture.

Theorem 8.5

(
[
JNS
]
)
.

Assume that
p
splits in
K
and that Hypothesis
(HS)
is satisfied, and let
\bm{\kappa}
be a
\Lambda
-adic anticyclotomic Euler system for
T
.

(a)

If
\kappa_{0}\neq 0
, then
\mathrm{Sel}_{\Gr}(K,A)
has
\mathcal{O}
-corank one,
\mathrm{Sel}_{\Gr}(K,T)
has
\mathcal{O}
-rank one, and

{\rm length}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathrm{Sel}_{\Gr}(K,A)_{/{\rm div}})\leq 2\;{\rm length}_{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{Sel}_{\Gr}(K,T)}{\mathcal{O}\cdot\kappa_{0}}\right),

where
(-)_{/{\rm div}}
denotes the quotient of
(-)
by its maximal divisible submodule.

(b)

If
\kappa_{\infty}
is not
\Lambda_{\ac}
-torsion, then
X_{\Gr}(K_{\infty},A)
and
\mathrm{Sel}_{\Gr}(K_{\infty},T)
have both
\Lambda_{\ac}
-rank one, and

\Char_{\Lambda_{\ac}}(X_{\Gr}(K_{\infty},A)_{\tors})\supset\Char_{\Lambda_{\ac}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{Sel}_{\Gr}(K_{\infty},T)}{\Lambda_{\ac}\cdot\kappa_{\infty}}\right)^{2},

where
(-)_{\rm tors}
denotes the maximal
\Lambda_{\ac}
-torsion submodule of
(-)
.

8.2.
Big image results

We now give conditions under which the hypotheses in the general results of
\lx@sectionsign\ref{sec:ES}
are verified in our setting. To that end, we shall build on
[
Loe17
]
.

As before, let
K/\mathbb{Q}
be an imaginary quadratic field of discriminant
-D
, let
(g,h)
be a pair of newforms of weights
(l,m)
of the same parity, levels
(N_{g},N_{h})
and characters
(\chi_{g},\chi_{h})
, and let
\psi
be a Grössencharacter of
K
of infinity type
(1-k,0)
for some positive even integer
k
and of conductor
\mathfrak{f}
. We denote by
\chi
the unique Dirichlet character modulo
N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathfrak{f})
such that
\psi((n))=n^{k-1}\chi(n)
for integers
n
coprime to
N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathfrak{f})
, and we assume that
\chi\varepsilon_{K}\chi_{g}\chi_{h}=1
.

We now make the further assumptions that:

•

neither
g
nor
h
are of CM type,

•

g
is not Galois-conjugate to a twist of
h
.

As in
[
Loe17
, §3.1]
, we define the open subgroups
H_{g}
and
H_{h}
of
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
, the quaternion algebras
B_{g}
and
B_{h}
, and the algebraic groups
G_{g}
and
G_{h}
, and put

B=B_{g}\times B_{h},\quad G=G_{g}\times_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}G_{h}.

We define
H
to be the intersection of
H_{g}
,
H_{h}
and
G_{K(\mathfrak{f})^{\circ}}
. (Note that in
loc. cit.

H
is defined to be the intersection of
H_{g}
and
H_{h}
, so our
H
might be a finite index subgroup of his
H
, but this will not affect the results that follow.)
We have an adelic representation
\tilde{\rho}_{g,h}:H\rightarrow G(\hat{\mathbb{Q}})
, and representations

\tilde{\rho}_{g,h,p}:H\longrightarrow G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})

for every rational prime
p
, and, by
[
Loe17
, Thm. 3.2.2]
,
\tilde{\rho}_{g,h,p}(H)=G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
for all but finitely many
p
.

Remark 8.6
.

Note that the representations studied in
[
Loe17
]
are the dual to the ones studied in this paper,
but as pointed out in
[
Loe17
, Rmk. 2.1.2]
, this difference is unimportant when considering the image.

Let
L
be a finite extension of
K
containing the Fourier coefficients of
g
and
h
and the image of
\psi
. Let
\mathfrak{P}
be a prime of
L
above some rational prime
p
and let
E=L_{\mathfrak{P}}
.

Definition 8.7
.

We say that the prime
\mathfrak{P}
is
good
if the following conditions hold:

•

p\geq 7
;

•

p
is unramified in
B
;

•

p
is coprime to
\mathfrak{f}
,
N_{g}
and
N_{h}
;

•

\tilde{\rho}_{g,h,p}(H)=G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
;

•

E=\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

Remark 8.8
.

Observe that all but the last condition exclude only finitely many primes. The last condition could be somewhat relaxed in some cases, and will be used largely for simplicity. Note also that the above set of conditions holds for a set of primes of positive density.

From now on, we assume that both
g
and
h
are ordinary, non-Eisenstein, and distinguished with respect to
\mathfrak{P}
.

Lemma 8.9
.

Assume that there is at least one prime which divides
D
but not
N_{g}
and one prime which divides
D
but not
N_{h}
. Then, if
\mathfrak{P}
is a good prime,

(\rho_{g,\mathfrak{P}}\times\rho_{h,\mathfrak{P}})(H\cap G_{K(p^{\infty})^{\circ}})=\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\times\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}).

Proof.

Let
\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{g})
and
\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{h})
be the Galois extensions of
\mathbb{Q}
cut out by the representations
\rho_{g}
and
\rho_{h}
attached to
g
and
h
, respectively. These extensions are unramified outside
pN_{g}
and
pN_{h}
, respectively. Therefore, the condition on
D
implies that
K\cap\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{g})=\mathbb{Q}
and
K\cap\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{h})=\mathbb{Q}
. Moreover, since any Galois extension of
\mathbb{Q}
contained in
K_{\infty}
must itself contain
K
, we also have
K_{\infty}\cap\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{g})=\mathbb{Q}
and
K_{\infty}\cap\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{h})=\mathbb{Q}
.

The conditions on
\mathfrak{P}
imply that

(\rho_{g,\mathfrak{P}}\times\rho_{h,\mathfrak{P}})(H\cap G_{\mathbb{Q}(\mu_{p^{\infty}})})=\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\times\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}),

and, from the remarks in the previous paragraph, it follows that

(\rho_{g,\mathfrak{P}}\times\rho_{h,\mathfrak{P}})(H\cap G_{K_{\infty}(\mu_{p^{\infty}})})=\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\times\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}).

Finally, since
H\cap G_{K(p^{\infty})^{\circ}}
is a normal subgroup of
H\cap G_{K_{\infty}(\mu_{p^{\infty}})}
of index dividing
p-1
and there are no such subgroups in
\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\times\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, the lemma follows.
∎

Now we are able to give conditions under which the results of
[
JNS
]
can be applied to our setting, i.e., to the representation
T_{g,h}^{\psi}
defined above.

Proposition 8.10
.

Assume that there is at least one prime which divides
D
but not
N_{g}
and one prime which divides
D
but not
N_{h}
. Let
\mathfrak{P}
be a good prime. Suppose that there exists
\sigma\in G_{K(p^{\infty})^{\circ}}
such that
\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}(\sigma)\neq\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}^{c}(\sigma)
modulo
p
. Then, hypotheses (HS) hold for
T_{g,h}^{\psi}
.

Proof.

Since
\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}

is trivial when restricted to
H\cap G_{K(p^{\infty})^{\circ}}
, condition (1’) follows easily from the previous lemma.

To prove condition (2’), we closely follow the proof of
[
Loe17
, Prop. 4.2.1]
. Write
\chi_{g}(\sigma)
and
\chi_{h}(\sigma)
for the images of
\sigma
by
\chi_{g}
and
\chi_{h}
via the natural identifications
\mathrm{Gal\,}(\mathbb{Q}(\mu_{N_{g}})/\mathbb{Q})\cong(\mathbb{Z}/N_{g}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}
and
\mathrm{Gal\,}(\mathbb{Q}(\mu_{N_{h}})/\mathbb{Q})\cong(\mathbb{Z}/N_{h}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}
. Then, by the previous lemma, the image of
\sigma H\cap G_{K(p^{\infty})^{\circ}}
under
\rho_{g,\mathfrak{P}}\times\rho_{h,\mathfrak{P}}
contains all the elements of the form

\left(\begin{pmatrix}x&0\\
0&x^{-1}\chi_{g}(\sigma)\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}y&0\\
0&y^{-1}\chi_{h}(\sigma)\end{pmatrix}\right),\quad x,y\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}.

Now choose
x\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
such that
x^{-2}\chi_{g}(\sigma)\neq 1\pmod{p}
and
x^{2}\chi_{h}(\sigma)\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}(\sigma)^{-2}\neq 1\pmod{p}
, which is possible since
p\geq 7
, and let
y=x^{-1}\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}(\sigma)
. Choose
\sigma_{0}\in\sigma H\cap G_{K(p^{\infty})^{\circ}}
whose image under
\rho_{g,\mathfrak{P}}\times\rho_{h,\mathfrak{P}}
is given by the element above, with the choices of
x
and
y
which we have just specified. Then, the eigenvalues of
\sigma_{0}
acting on
T_{g,h}^{\psi}
are 1,
x^{-2}\chi_{g}(\sigma)
,
x^{2}\chi_{h}(\sigma)\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}(\sigma)^{-2}
and
\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}^{c}(\sigma)\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}(\sigma)^{-1}
, which proves condition (2’).

To check condition (3’), we can argue as in
[
KLZ17
, Rmk. 11.1.3]
. By the previous lemma, we can find an element
\tau_{0}\in H\cap G_{K(p^{\infty})^{\circ}}
such that

(\rho_{g,\mathfrak{P}}\times\rho_{h,\mathfrak{P}})(\tau_{0})=\left(\begin{pmatrix}-1&0\\
0&-1\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\right),

so
\tau_{0}
acts on
T_{g,h}^{\psi}
as multiplication by
-1
.

Finally, condition (4’) follows from the assumption that
g
and
h
are non-Eisenstein and
p
-distinguished.
∎

Remark 8.11
.

If we are just interested in ensuring that hypotheses (HW) hold for
T_{g,h}^{\psi}
, we can relax some of the assumptions above. For example,
we do not need to require
g
and
h
to be non-Eisenstein, and
we can require that there exist
\sigma\in G_{K(1)^{\circ}(\mu_{p^{\infty}})}
such that
\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}(\sigma)\neq\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}^{c}(\sigma)
, without requiring this inequality to hold modulo
p
.

9.
Proof of Theorems
B
,
C
, and
D

Let the setting be as in the Introduction. In particular,
g\in S_{l}(N_{g},\chi_{g})
and
h\in S_{m}(N_{h},\chi_{h})
are newforms of weights
l\geq m\geq 2
of the same parity,
K/\mathbb{Q}
is an imaginary quadratic field of discriminant
-D<0
,

\psi
is a Grössencharacter for
K
of infinity type
(1-k,0)
for some even integer
k\geq 2
, and we consider the
G_{K}
-representation

V_{g,h}^{\psi}=V_{g}\otimes_{E}V_{h}(\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}^{-1})(1-c),

where
c=(k+l+m-2)/2
.

Lemma 9.1
.

The Bloch–Kato Selmer group of
V_{g,h}^{\psi}
is given by

\mathrm{Sel}(K,V_{g,h}^{\psi})\cong\begin{cases}\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(K,V_{g,h}^{\psi})&\textrm{if $l-m<k<l+m$,}\\[1.99997pt]
\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}}(K,V_{g,h}^{\psi})&\textrm{if $k\geq l+m$.}\end{cases}

Proof.

Note that by Shapiro’s lemma
H^{1}(K,V_{g,h}^{\psi})\cong H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},V_{fgh})
, where
f=\theta_{\psi}
is the theta series of
\psi
and
V_{fgh}
is the specialization of the big Galois representation
\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}{\mathbf{g}}{\mathbf{h}}}^{\dagger}
in (
7.1
) to weights
(k,l,m)
.
One immediately checks that the Hodge–Tate weights of the
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-subrepresentation
\mathscr{F}^{2}V_{fgh}\subset V_{fgh}
(resp.
V_{fgh}^{f}\subset V_{fgh}
) are all
<0
(with the
p
-adic cyclotomic character
\epsilon_{\rm cyc}
having Hodge–Tate weight
-1
) if and only if
l-m<k<l+m
(resp.
k\geq l+m
). The result follows.
∎

Here we collect a set of hypotheses for our later reference. For any nonzero
m\in\mathbb{Z}
,
{\rm prime}(m)
denotes the set of primes that divide
m
, and
{\rm prime}^{c}(m)
its complement.

Hypotheses 9.2
.

(h1)

g
and
h
are ordinary at
p
, non-Eisenstein, and
p
-distinguished.

(h2)

p
splits in
K
,

(h3)

p
does not divide the class number of
K
,

(h4)

\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}|_{G_{K(p^{\infty})^{\circ}}}\neq\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}^{c}|_{G_{K(p^{\infty})^{\circ}}}
modulo
p
,

(h5)

neither
g
nor
h
are of CM type,

(h6)

g
is not Galois-conjugate to a twist of
h
.

(h7)

{\rm prime}(D)\cap{\rm prime}^{c}(N_{g})\neq\emptyset
and
{\rm prime}(D)\cap{\rm prime}^{c}(N_{h})\neq\emptyset
,

(h8)

\mathfrak{P}
is a good prime in the sense of Definition
8.7
.

9.1.
Proof of Theorem
B

Let
{\kappa_{\psi,g,h,1,\infty}}\in H^{1}_{\rm Iw}(K[p^{\infty}],T_{g,h}^{\psi})
be the Iwasawa cohomology class of conductor
n=1
from Theorem
6.5
, and set

(9.1)

{\kappa_{\psi,g,h}}={\kappa_{\psi,g,h,1}}\in H^{1}(K,T_{g,h}^{\psi}),

where
{\kappa_{\psi,g,h,1}}={\rm pr}_{K}({\kappa_{\psi,g,h,1,\infty}})
.

If
l-m<k<l+m
, the next result recovers Theorem
B
in the Introduction. Note however, that the result does not require these inequalities to hold.

Theorem 9.3
.

Assume hypotheses (h1)–(h8). Then the following implication holds:

{\kappa_{\psi,g,h}}\neq 0\quad\Longrightarrow\quad{\rm dim}_{E}\,\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(K,V^{\psi}_{g,h})=1.

In particular, if
l-m<k<l+m
and
{\kappa_{\psi,g,h}}\neq 0
then the Bloch–Kato Selmer group
\mathrm{Sel}(K,V^{\psi}_{g,h})
is one-dimensional.

Proof.

By Proposition
6.6
, the classes
{\kappa_{\psi,g,h,n}}:={\rm pr}_{K[n]}({\kappa_{\psi,g,h,n,\infty}})
land in
\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(K[n],T_{g,h}^{\psi})
, and by Theorem
6.5
they form an anticyclotomic Euler system for
V_{g,h}^{\psi}
. Therefore, the result follows from Theorem
8.3
and Proposition
8.10
.
∎

Remark 9.4
.

If
k=2
and
l=m\geq 2
, working with the classes
{\kappa_{\psi,g,h,n}}
from Theorem
4.6
, rather than those from Theorem
6.5
as above, hypotheses (h2)-(h3) in Theorem
9.3
can be replaced by the assumption that there exists an element
\gamma\in G_{K}
satisfying the conditions in Remark
8.4
. Further, (h1) and (h4) can be relaxed as discussed in Remark
8.11
.

9.2.
Proof of Theorem
C

Recall that
\theta_{\psi}\in S_{k}(N_{\psi},\chi\varepsilon_{K})
is the theta series attached to
\psi
, and put
N=\lcm(N_{\psi},N_{g},N_{h})
.

The next theorem, establishing cases of the Bloch–Kato conjecture for
V_{g,h}^{\psi}
in analytic rank zero, recovers Theorem
C
in the Introduction.

Theorem 9.5
.

Assume hypotheses (h1)–(h8), and in addition that:

•

\varepsilon_{\ell}(\theta_{\psi},g,h)=+1
for all primes
\ell\mid N
,

•

{\rm gcd}(N_{\psi},N_{g},N_{h})
is squarefree.

If
k\geq l+m
then the following implication holds:

L(V^{\psi}_{g,h},0)\neq 0\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\mathrm{Sel}(K,V^{\psi}_{g,h})=0.

Proof.

We continue to denote by
{\kappa_{\psi,g,h}}
the image of the class in (
9.1
) under the isomorphism

H^{1}(K,V_{g,h}^{\psi})\cong H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},V_{fgh})

coming from Shapiro’s lemma. If
k\geq l+m
, the central value
L(V^{\psi}_{g,h},0)
is in the range of interpolation of the triple product
p
-adic
L
-function of Theorem
7.1
, and so by Proposition
7.3
and Theorem
7.4
its non-vanishing implies that the image of
{\kappa_{\psi,g,h}}
under the natural map

{\rm res}_{p}:\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(\mathbb{Q},V_{fgh})\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{gh})

is nonzero. In particular,
{\kappa_{\psi,g,h}}\neq 0
, and therefore by Theorem
9.3
the balanced Selmer group
\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(K,V_{g,h}^{\psi})=\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(\mathbb{Q},V_{fgh})
is one-dimensional.

From the exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}\cap+}(\mathbb{Q},V_{fgh})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(\mathbb{Q},V_{fgh})

\displaystyle\overset{{\rm res}_{p}}{\longrightarrow}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{gh})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}\cup+}(\mathbb{Q},V_{fgh})^{\vee}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(\mathbb{Q},V_{fgh})^{\vee}\longrightarrow 0

coming from global duality (adopting notations similar to those in Theorem
7.15
), we thus see that
\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}\cap+}(\mathbb{Q},V_{fgh})=0
and that
\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}\cup+}(\mathbb{Q},V_{fgh})=\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(\mathbb{Q},V_{fgh})
.
Together with the exact sequence

\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}\cup+}(\mathbb{Q},V_{fgh})\xrightarrow{{\rm res}_{p}}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{gh})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{Q},V_{fgh})^{\vee}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}\cap+}(\mathbb{Q},V_{fgh})^{\vee}\longrightarrow 0,

it follows that
\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{Q},V_{fgh})=0
, and combined with Lemma
9.1
this concludes the proof.
∎

Refining the proof of Theorem
9.5
, we can further bound the size of the Bloch–Kato Selmer group for the discrete module
A_{g,h}^{\psi}=V^{\psi}_{g,h}/T^{\psi}_{g,h}
in terms of
L
-values. For the statement, let
{\mathbf{f}}
be the Hida family associated to
\psi
as in
\lx@sectionsign\ref{subsec:Iw}
, so that
{\mathbf{f}}_{k}
is the ordinary
p
-stabilization of
\theta_{\psi}
, and, keeping with the notations in Theorem
7.1
, put
\alpha_{k}=\psi(\overline{\mathfrak{p}})
and
\beta_{k}=\psi(\mathfrak{p})
. Let also
\varepsilon_{\ell}(\theta_{\psi},g,h)=\varepsilon_{\ell}(V_{fgh})
denote the epsilon factor associated to
V_{fgh}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}}}
, where
f=\theta_{\psi}
.

Theorem 9.6
.

Assume hypotheses (h1)–(h8), and in addition that:

•

\varepsilon_{\ell}(\theta_{\psi},g,h)=+1
for all primes
\ell\mid N
,

•

{\rm gcd}(N_{\psi},N_{g},N_{h})
is squarefree,

•

H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}^{gh})
is torsion-free,

•

H^{1}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{fgh})
is torsion-free for
\mathcal{L}\in\{\bal,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}\cap+,\mathcal{F}\cup+\}
.

If
k\geq l+m
and
L(V^{\psi}_{g,h},0)\neq 0
then the
\mathcal{O}
-module
\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}}(K,A^{\psi}_{g,h})
is finite and

{\rm length}_{{\mathcal{O}}}(\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}}(K,A^{\psi}_{g,h}))\leq 2\,v_{\mathfrak{P}}\left(\frac{(l-2)!(m-2)!}{(k-c-1)!}\cdot\frac{\mathcal{E}_{1}({\mathbf{f}}_{k})}{\mathcal{E}({\mathbf{f}}_{k},g,h)}\cdot\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\xi}(\underline{\breve{{\mathbf{f}}}},\underline{\breve{g}},\underline{\breve{h}})(k)\right),

where
\mathcal{E}_{1}({\mathbf{f}}_{k})=\bigl(1-\frac{\beta_{k}}{p\alpha_{k}}\bigr)
,
\mathcal{E}({\mathbf{f}}_{k},g,h)=\bigl(1-\frac{\alpha_{k}\alpha_{g}\alpha_{h}}{p^{c}}\bigr)\bigl(1-\frac{\beta_{k}\beta_{g}\alpha_{h}}{p^{c}}\bigr)\bigl(1-\frac{\beta_{k}\alpha_{g}\beta_{h}}{p^{c}}\bigr)\bigl(1-\frac{\beta_{k}\beta_{g}\beta_{h}}{p^{c}}\bigr)
, and
c=(k+l+m-2)/2
.

Proof.

As in the proof of Theorem
9.5
, if
k\geq l+m
and
L(V^{\psi}_{g,h},0)\neq 0
then the class
{\kappa_{\psi,g,h}}
is nonzero. Since by Theorem
6.5
this is the bottom class of an anticyclotomic Euler system for
V_{gh}^{\psi}
, from Theorem
8.5
and
Proposition
8.10
we deduce that
\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(K,A^{\psi}_{g,h})
has
\mathcal{O}
-corank one, with

(9.2)

{\rm length}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(K,A^{\psi}_{g,h})_{/{\rm div}})\leq 2\;{\rm length}_{\mathcal{O}}\biggl(\frac{\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(K,T_{g,h}^{\psi})}{\mathcal{O}\cdot{\kappa_{\psi,g,h}}}\biggr).

By the exact sequence (
7.8
) specialized to weight
k
, it follows that
\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}\cup+}(K,A_{g,h}^{\psi})
has also
\mathcal{O}
-corank one. Thus both
\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(K,T_{g,h}^{\psi})\subset\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}\cup+}(K,T_{g,h}^{\psi})
have
{\mathcal{O}}
-rank one, and therefore

(9.3)

\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(K,T_{g,h}^{\psi})=\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}\cup+}(K,T_{g,h}^{\psi}),

since their quotient is
{\mathcal{O}}
-torsion free. Moreover, letting
\pi\in\mathcal{O}
be a uniformizer, as in the proof of Lemma
7.13
we find that

\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}\cup+}(K,A_{g,h}^{\psi})[\pi^{i}]\cong E/{\mathcal{O}}[\pi^{i}]\oplus\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}\cap+}(K,A_{g,h}^{\psi})[\pi^{i}]

for all
i
, and hence
{\rm length}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\rm Sel}_{\mathcal{F}\cup+}(K,A_{g,h}^{\psi})_{/{\rm div}})={\rm length}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\rm Sel}_{\mathcal{F}\cap+}(K,A_{g,h}^{\psi}))
.

The finiteness of
\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}}(K,A_{g,h}^{\psi})
with the stated bound on its
{\mathcal{O}}
-length thus follows from (
9.2
) by the same argument as in the proof of Theorem
7.15
, noting that by Theorem
7.4
and the same calculation as in
[
BSV22
, §8.5]
(see esp. the equality following [
op. cit.
, (189)]) the map

\xi_{k}\cdot\left\langle{\rm exp}^{*}_{p}(-),\eta_{\breve{f}}\otimes\omega_{\breve{g}}\otimes\omega_{\breve{h}}\right\rangle

where
f=\theta_{\psi}
and
\xi_{k}
is the weight
k
specialization of the congruence ideal generator
\xi\in\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}
, gives an isomorphism
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}^{gh})\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}
taking
{\kappa_{\psi,g,h}}
to

\frac{(l-2)!\cdot(m-2)!}{(k-c-1)!}\cdot\frac{\mathcal{E}_{0}({\mathbf{f}}_{k})\cdot\mathcal{E}_{1}({\mathbf{f}}_{k})}{\mathcal{E}({\mathbf{f}}_{k},g,h)}\cdot\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\xi}(\underline{\breve{{\mathbf{f}}}},\underline{\breve{g}},\underline{\breve{h}})(k),

where
\mathcal{E}_{0}({\mathbf{f}}_{k})=\bigl(1-\frac{\beta_{k}}{\alpha_{k}}\bigr)
is a
p
-adic unit.

More precisely, under the freeness assumption in the statement, the weight
k
specializations of (
7.8
) and (
7.9
) yield the exact sequences

(9.4)

0\longrightarrow{\rm coker}({\rm res}_{p})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}\cup+}(K,A_{g,h}^{\psi})^{\vee}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(K,A_{g,h}^{\psi})^{\vee}\longrightarrow 0,

0\longrightarrow{\rm coker}({\rm res}_{p})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}}(K,A_{g,h}^{\psi})^{\vee}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}\cap+}(K,A_{g,h}^{\psi})^{\vee}\longrightarrow 0,

where the two terms
{\rm coker}({\rm res}_{p})
are equal in light of (
9.3
). Thus we find

\displaystyle{\rm lt}_{{\mathcal{O}}}(\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}}(K,A_{g,h}^{\psi}))={\rm lt}_{{\mathcal{O}}}(\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}}(K,A_{g,h}^{\psi})^{\vee})

\displaystyle={\rm lt}_{{\mathcal{O}}}(\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}\cap+}(K,A_{g,h}^{\psi})^{\vee})+{\rm lt}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\rm coker}({\rm res}_{p}))

\displaystyle={\rm lt}_{{\mathcal{O}}}((\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}\cup+}(K,A_{g,h}^{\psi})_{/{\rm div}})^{\vee})+{\rm lt}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\rm coker}({\rm res}_{p}))

\displaystyle={\rm lt}_{{\mathcal{O}}}((\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(K,A_{g,h}^{\psi})_{/{\rm div}})^{\vee})+2\,{\rm lt}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\rm coker}({\rm res}_{p}))

\displaystyle={\rm lt}_{{\mathcal{O}}}(\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(K,A_{g,h}^{\psi})_{/{\rm div}})+2\,{\rm lt}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\rm coker}({\rm res}_{p})),

where the third equality follows from (
9.4
) and Lemma
9.7
below, concluding the proof.
∎

Lemma 9.7
.

Let
0\rightarrow A\xrightarrow{j}B\rightarrow C\rightarrow 0
be an exact sequence of finitely generated
{\mathcal{O}}
-modules, and assume that
A
is finite. Then
B_{\rm tors}/j(A)\cong C_{\rm tors}
.

In particular, if
B^{\prime},C^{\prime}
are cofinitely generated
{\mathcal{O}}
-modules and we have an exact sequence
0\rightarrow A\xrightarrow{j}(B^{\prime})^{\vee}\rightarrow(C^{\prime})^{\vee}\rightarrow 0
with
A
finite, then

(B^{\prime}_{/{\rm div}})^{\vee}/j(A)\cong(C^{\prime}_{/{\rm div}})^{\vee},

and so
{\rm lt}_{{\mathcal{O}}}((B^{\prime}_{/{\rm div}})^{\vee})={\rm lt}_{{\mathcal{O}}}(A)+{\rm lt}_{{\mathcal{O}}}((C^{\prime}_{/{\rm div}})^{\vee})
.

Proof.

Writing
B\cong{\mathcal{O}}^{r}\oplus B_{\rm tors}
,
C\cong{\mathcal{O}}^{s}\oplus C_{\rm tors}
we have, by the finiteness of
A
,
r=s
and
j(A)\subset B_{\rm tors}
, so

{\mathcal{O}}^{r}\oplus C_{\rm tors}\cong C\cong B/j(A)\cong{\mathcal{O}}^{r}\oplus(B_{\rm tors}/j(A)),

which implies the result.
∎

Remark 9.8
.

The condition that
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}^{gh})
is torsion-free is equivalent to the vanishing of
H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},A_{f}^{gh})
, which is satisfied if
k+2\neq l+m
modulo
2(p-1)
or if
\chi_{f}(p)\alpha_{g}\alpha_{h}/\alpha_{k}\neq 1
modulo
p
. Similarly, the last condition in the statement of Theorem
9.6
can be recast in terms of the vanishing of the corresponding
0
-th cohomology groups.

Remark 9.9
.

By Theorem
7.1
, the non-vanishing of
L(V^{\psi}_{g,h},0)
implies that
\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\xi}(\underline{\breve{{\mathbf{f}}}},\underline{\breve{g}},\underline{\breve{h}})(x)\neq 0
, so the upper bound provided by Theorem
9.6
is non-trivial. Moreover, by the interpolation formula in Theorem
7.1
, this upper bound can be expressed in terms of the central
L
-value
L(V^{\psi}_{g,h},0)
, thus giving a result towards the Tamagawa number conjecture of
[
BK90
]
.

9.3.
Proof of Theorem
D

As before, let
{\mathbf{f}}
be the Hida family attached to
\psi
as in
\lx@sectionsign\ref{subsec:Iw}
. Let
{\kappa_{\psi,g,h,1,\infty}}
be the
\Lambda
-adic class of conductor
n=1
constructed in Theorem
6.5
, and set

{\kappa_{\psi,g,h,\infty}}:={\kappa_{\psi,g,h,1,\infty}}\in H^{1}_{\rm Iw}(K_{\infty},T_{g,h}^{\psi}).

As noted before the proof of Proposition
6.6
, under the Shapiro isomorphism

H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})\cong H^{1}(K,\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}}(\bm{\kappa}_{\text{ac}}^{-1})\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}T^{\psi}_{g,h})\cong H^{1}_{\rm Iw}(K_{\infty},T^{\psi}_{g,h}),

the balanced Selmer group
\mathrm{Sel}_{\rm bal}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{\dagger})
of
\lx@sectionsign\ref{subsec:IMC}
is identified with the Greenberg Selmer group
\mathrm{Sel}_{\rm Gr}(K_{\infty},T^{\psi}_{g,h})
of
\lx@sectionsign\ref{sec:ES}
attached to
G_{K_{v}}
-invariant subspaces
\mathcal{F}_{v}^{+}(V_{g,h}^{\psi})\subset V_{g,h}^{\psi}
in (
4.1
) at the primes
v\mid p
. Moreover, under this isomorphism, the class
\kappa({\mathbf{f}},g,h)
in
\lx@sectionsign\ref{subsec:ERL}

corresponds to the class
{\kappa_{\psi,g,h,\infty}}
.

The next result, establishing one of the divisibilities predicted by the Iwasawa main conjectures from
\lx@sectionsign\ref{subsec:IMC}
, recovers Theorem
D
in the Introduction.

Theorem 9.10
.

Assume hypotheses (h1)–(h8), and in addition that:

•

\varepsilon_{\ell}(\theta_{\psi},g,h)=+1
for all primes
\ell\mid N
,

•

{\rm gcd}(N_{\psi},N_{g},N_{h})
is squarefree.

If
\kappa({\mathbf{f}},g,h)
is not
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}
-torsion, then the following hold:

(a)

The modules
\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})
and
X_{\bal}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})
have both
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}
-rank one, and

\Char_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}}(X_{\bal}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})_{\tors})\supset\Char_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{Sel}_{\bal}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})}{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}\cdot\kappa({\mathbf{f}},g,h)}\right)^{2}.

(b)

The modules
\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})
and
X_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}})
are both
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}
-torsion, and

\Char_{\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}}(X_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{A}_{{\mathbf{f}}gh}^{{\dagger}}))\supset(L_{p}({\mathbf{f}},g,h))

in
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

Proof.

The non-triviality assumption on
\kappa({\mathbf{f}},g,h)
implies that
{\kappa_{\psi,g,h,\infty}}
is not
\Lambda_{{\mathbf{f}}}
-torsion.
Since by Theorem
6.5
the class
{\kappa_{\psi,g,h,\infty}}
is the bottom class of a
\Lambda
-adic Euler system for
V_{g,h}^{\psi}
, part (a) follows from Theorem
8.5
and Proposition
8.10
. By Theorem
7.15
, part (b) of the theorem follows from part (a), so this concludes the proof.
∎
